- *Kokkonen v. Guardian Life Insurance Co. of America*, 511 U.S. 375 (1994), is distinguished: jurisdiction over a new settlement-enforcement controversy is different from disposition of a judgment already lawfully under review.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Court neither enforces the settlement nor supplies a ruling on bankruptcy priority, plan confirmation or the former automatic-stay dispute. The authority under §2106 to make an equitable disposition of a judgment lawfully before an appellate court differs from authority to reopen a completed judgment after the ordinary review process has ended. This decision does not create a new collateral reopening procedure, displace independently applicable Rule 60 requirements, or make a settlement agreement itself sufficient grounds for vacatur.

##### Voluntary settlement ordinarily does not justify vacatur

**Holding:** A party that voluntarily settles while pursuing review does not ordinarily establish equitable entitlement to vacatur of the judgment it sought to overturn; the movant must show a further sufficient equitable reason. Exceptional settlement circumstances remain possible, but a settlement term calling for vacatur is not itself such a circumstance; Bancorp’s showing establishes none, so vacatur is denied.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Scalia), unanimous.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- *United States v. Munsingwear, Inc.*, 340 U.S. 36 (1950), preserves equitable relief when review is frustrated by happenstance; its actual refusal to erase an unchallenged judgment does not create automatic vacatur after settlement.
- *Karcher v. May*, 484 U.S. 72 (1987), extends its reason for denying vacatur after voluntary abandonment of review to the voluntary settlement here, while preserving its distinct party-capacity setting.
- *Izumi Seimitsu Kogyo Kabushiki Kaisha v. U.S. Philips Corp.*, 510 U.S. 27 (1993), remains a threshold decision that left settlement vacatur undecided. Its separate vacatur discussion supplies no earlier controlling rule.
- *Digital Equipment Corp. v. Desktop Direct, Inc.*, 511 U.S. 863 (1994), remains a collateral-order decision; it does not establish a private right to erase a judgment following settlement.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Mootness caused by circumstances beyond the movant’s choice, including unilateral action of the prevailing party, remains distinct. No categorical ban on exceptional settlement relief or universal rule of collateral estoppel is adopted. No Rule 60(b) relief or remand to seek it is ordered.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Vacatur is denied and the Supreme Court proceeding dismissed as moot. The Ninth Circuit judgment remains undisturbed without endorsement of its bankruptcy merits; no other order or underlying Chapter 11 proceeding is vacated or dismissed, and no settlement-enforcement or Rule 60(b) remand is ordered.

### Ripeness and vacatur after loss of federal approval

#### Anderson v. Green, 513 U.S. 557

**Docket or dockets:** No. 94-197.
**Decided:** February 22, 1995.

##### Present ripeness after loss of required federal approval

**Holding:** A constitutional challenge to this approval-dependent benefit program is not ripe when the necessary federal authorization has been removed and no replacement authorization is established. The mere possibility of a new approval does not permit adjudication of the constitutionality of a program whose operative terms remain contingent.

**Proposition-level authority:** Per curiam opinion of the Court, unanimous.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Regional Rail Reorganization Act Cases is applied to present ripeness. Hall v. Beals is applied to conjectural future controversies. Church of Scientology is distinguished because identified effectual relief there persisted; its partial-relief rule remains intact. Carroll v. President and Commissioners of Princess Anne is distinguished because its continuing events and effects were demonstrated. Minnesota v. Dickerson retains its demand for actual continuing legal consequences; none is invented here.

##### Vacatur of the preliminary judgments

**Holding:** When the separate judicial removal of an indispensable federal approval prevents review of these constitutional preliminary judgments, equitable vacatur clears the judgments without deciding their merits. The Ninth Circuit judgment is vacated, and that court must direct vacatur of the District Court judgment and dismissal of the present action without prejudice to a challenge to a later legally operative program.

**Proposition-level authority:** Per curiam opinion of the Court, unanimous.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** United States v. Munsingwear is applied to the judgment-clearing purpose of vacatur. U.S. Bancorp is applied to the distinction between merits power and equitable disposition and to the significance of voluntary surrender; its ordinary settlement rule is neither displaced nor made automatic outside settlement.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The Ninth Circuit judgment is vacated, with instructions to vacate the District Court's preliminary judgment and dismiss without prejudice as unripe. No constitutional benefits ruling, damages, federal approval or authority to implement the cap is supplied; a later legally operative program may be challenged.

### Mootness and seasonal permits

#### Capitol Square Review and Advisory Board v. Pinette

**Docket or dockets:** No. 94-780.
**Decided:** June 29, 1995.

For the other questions in Capitol Square Review and Advisory Board v. Pinette, decided June 29, 1995, No. 94-780. see First Amendment — Private religious displays in public forums (Ohio’s categorical Establishment Clause defense cannot sustain this exclusion).

##### The expired permit dispute remains reviewable

**Holding:** A dispute over a short seasonal permit remains live when its duration prevents full review and there is a reasonable expectation that the same claimant will again face the challenged restriction. These applicants meet both requirements; recurrence affecting other speakers alone would not suffice.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stone, Part I, joined by Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer: nine votes for this threshold holding.

**Limits and questions not reached:** A broader prayer alone does not establish renewed exposure. Reviewability neither revives the expired permit nor extends its relief to every future application.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Weinstein v. Bradford: applies both short duration and same-claimant recurrence; the exception does not extend to every recurring public issue.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Affirm the Sixth Circuit at the original scope of the Count One merits judgment: the December 21–24, 1993 permit period remains expired. No new permit, extension, sign condition, removal order, damages or remand is entered. Unappealed rally, damages and other matters remain outside review.

#### Chabad-Lubavitch of Georgia v. Miller

**Docket or dockets:** No. 93-1047.
**Decided:** June 29, 1995.

For the other questions in Chabad-Lubavitch of Georgia v. Miller, decided June 29, 1995, No. 93-1047. see First Amendment — Private religious displays in public forums (Georgia’s categorical defense cannot sustain summary judgment excluding the private religious display; Interior location does not itself make clearly private expression governmental religion).

##### The recurring access dispute remains live, and only the Rotunda claim is reviewed

**Holding:** A short annual display dispute remains reviewable when full litigation cannot finish within the holiday and the same applicant reasonably expects to seek access again. Chabad’s repeated requests and stated intention to reapply satisfy that rule; this appeal reaches only Count II concerning the Rotunda, not the conceded outdoor-plaza claim.

**Proposition-level authority:** Souter, Part I, joined by Stone, Stevens, O’Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer: nine votes.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Repeated requests and a stated intention to reapply establish recurrence for Chabad. The conceded outdoor-plaza unattended-object restriction does not apply inside the Rotunda and is not reopened.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Weinstein v. Bradford and Murphy v. Hunt: apply short duration and same-party recurrence; the exception remains claimant-specific.
- Capitol Square Review and Advisory Board v. Pinette: applies the earlier decision’s limited reviewability rule without extending an expired permit.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Affirm the Eleventh Circuit reversal of State summary judgment on Count II for further proceedings and lawful relief on neutral terms. No Supreme Court permit, retrospective 1991 display, fixed sign, permanent seasonal entitlement, damages amount or compulsory forum closure is ordered. Count I remains undisturbed; lawful physical, safety, scheduling and identification rules and genuinely prospective viewpoint-neutral forum closure or redefinition remain possible.

### Relief-specific mootness

#### Hodge v. Jones

**Docket or dockets:** Fourth Circuit No. 93-1182; no separate Supreme Court docket supplied.
**Decided:** October 23, 1995.

##### Expunction and related declaratory relief after the applicable retention period expires

**Holding:** The presented requests for expunction and related declaratory relief are moot because the applicable five-year retention period expired in January 1994 and plaintiffs do not contend that required expunction then failed. That rule applied if no further incidents were reported involving the same alleged perpetrator; this disposition neither applies the later 120-day rule retroactively nor finds actual destruction from evidence outside the record.

**Proposition-level authority:** O'Connor's Part II, unanimous.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The surviving damages dispute does not maintain the separate prospective controversy. Contingent fear of future disclosure establishes neither a newly retained record nor a mootness exception. No fee entitlement or remedy for the unpleaded inaccurate-code episode is decided.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Dismiss the presented prospective requests as moot. The damages-immunity holding is separately recorded under Civil Rights, Hodge, October 23, 1995, Fourth Circuit No. 93-1182.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Ordinary live-controversy principles govern the specific relief sought; immunity is not a basis for prospective mootness.

### Intervening statutory change and mootness

#### United States v. Chesapeake & Potomac Telephone Co. of Virginia; National Cable Television Assn., Inc. v. Bell Atlantic Corp., 516 U.S. 415

**Docket or dockets:** Nos. 94-1893 and 94-1900.

**Decided:** February 27, 1996.

##### The case-specific threshold effect of intervening repeal after argument

**Holding:** Under 28 U.S.C. §2106, the repeal of the provision adjudicated below and its unresolved effect on the claims justify appellate vacatur and remand for consideration of the surviving controversy before constitutional adjudication. The disposition establishes neither final mootness nor categorical equitable-vacatur entitlement.

**Proposition-level authority:** Per curiam opinion of the Court; no individual lineup or numerical tally disclosed.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The old injunction does not automatically extend to the successor statute. No constitutional ruling on either scheme, final mootness, certification/compliance finding, damages entitlement or guaranteed amendment is made. The new open-video regime requires applicable regulatory compliance, certification and approval for reduced burdens; its rulemaking period establishes no blanket six-month prohibition. No statutory-change assumption alone resolves meaningful remaining relief.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Vacate the Fourth Circuit judgment in both dockets and remand there to consider mootness. Further district proceedings are permitted if needed, not compelled. If no controversy survives, appropriate dismissal and treatment of remaining orders must follow applicable vacatur law; otherwise proceedings remain confined to the surviving controversy. No present dismissal or vacatur of the district judgment or injunction is ordered.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Church of Scientology requires attention to meaningful partial relief; Northeastern Florida distinguishes continuing injury from a replacement ending it. Bancorp separates disposal authority from equitable entitlement and preserves its voluntary-settlement rule, exceptional-circumstances qualification and outside-choice distinction. Munsingwear supplies no missing mootness finding; Anderson v. Green's lost-approval setting and Lawrence's petition-stage reconsideration do not dictate this disposition.

### Census standing and review

#### Wisconsin v. City of New York; Oklahoma v. City of New York; Department of Commerce v. City of New York, 517 U.S. 1

**Docket or dockets:** Nos. 94-1614, 94-1631 and 94-1985.
**Decided:** March 20, 1996.

##### The California voter appellants have continuing representational injury and likely redress

**Holding:** The identified California voter appellants have standing where the challenged census determination has a concrete connection to an asserted lost California seat and authoritative review is substantially likely to afford redress through the allocation process. Their claim neither depends on California's appeal nor requires guaranteed selection of a dataset or an injunction against the President.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stevens's Part I, joined by all seven other Associates: eight Justices. Stone-Zsela does not join Part I.

**Material application:** Mack and Morales were identified individual voter appellants; the corrected adjustment identified a potential Wisconsin-to-California seat transfer. The Court need not resolve California's conflicting appellate designation, every other plaintiff's standing or independently establish unchanged residence through decision.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Montana recognizes continuing allocation injury and likely declaratory redress despite completed transmission; Franklin v. Massachusetts applies that principle to the responsible census determination. Neither guarantees a preferred methodological result.

##### APA finality does not reopen the unpresented ordinary APA merits

**Holding:** The Secretary's completed census determination remains final agency action under §704 despite later presidential transmission, but reviewability does not reopen the district court's ordinary APA disposition in this constitutional appeal. Constitutional reasonableness does not substitute for every APA requirement.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stevens's Part I, the same eight-Justice coalition.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No fresh APA merits decision, blanket forfeiture of statutory claims, President-as-agency ruling or coercive presidential relief is supplied.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Franklin preserves finality and a contemporaneous, reasonably discernible actual explanation. State Farm's relevant-factors and rational-explanation requirements, Bowman's discernible-path rule and Chenery/Boston & Maine's actual-ground constraint remain applicable to properly presented APA claims.

For Wisconsin's constitutional census-method holding and bounded three-docket remedy, March 20, 1996, Nos. 94-1614, 94-1631 and 94-1985, see Constitutional Structure — Census enumeration.

### Associational standing and statutory enforcement

#### United Food & Commercial Workers Union Local 751 v. Brown Group, Inc., 517 U.S. 544

**Docket or dockets:** No. 95-340.
**Decided:** May 13, 1996.

##### Express authority for representative enforcement of WARN monetary liability

**Holding:** Section 2104(a)(5), read with WARN's exclusive-representative definition and employee-liability provisions, authorizes the representative to enforce represented employees' back-pay and benefit entitlements. The provision supplies enforcement authority as well as venue; the union does not become the beneficiary of employee recovery or acquire the local government's distinct penalty claim.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Souter), Part I, unanimous.

**Material application:** Reading “aggrieved” to require every named plaintiff to be personally entitled to payment would deprive the enacted representative language of operative force. Deletion of an earlier union-specific remedy did not delete the authority actually enacted. This is express authority for the pleaded action, rather than a general implied cause of action.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Authorization enlarges no statutory remedy and establishes no violation, employee eligibility, amount, or entitlement to fees. WARN's exclusive remedies do not authorize an injunction against a closing or mass layoff. Statutory coverage, notice requirements and exceptions, employee-specific limits and reductions, defenses, and discretionary fee and good-faith reduction provisions retain their own predicates; none is adjudicated satisfied here.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** *Warth* and *Brock* did not decide Congress's express authorization of this representative damages action. *Varity* and *Meghrig* preserve the distinction between authorized enforcement and additional remedies without transplanting their different statutory relief.

##### Congress's displacement of Hunt's individual-participation condition

**Holding:** *Hunt*'s third condition—that neither the claim nor requested relief require individual-member participation—is prudential. Congress may displace it and has done so through WARN's express representative authorization. Represented employees must nevertheless establish concrete injury, traceability, and redressability with support appropriate to the litigation stage. Individual proof may remain necessary, but its potential necessity no longer categorically bars this authorized action.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part II of the same unanimous Court opinion.

**Material application:** Accepted allegations of employment loss and deficient notice, undisputed representative status, and relief payable to the employees establish the controversy at dismissal. Germaneness is satisfied. Different employment dates, wages, and medical expenses may require individual proof under applicable procedures; pleading sufficiency is not trial proof. Germaneness does not automatically establish adequate representation for binding absent persons.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Without clear congressional authorization, the third condition remains operative. Whether simplified damages could satisfy it without displacement and whether germaneness is itself prudential remain open. The Court prescribes no class certification, joinder, adequacy proceeding, jury arrangement, assignment, payroll-only proof rule, or universal employee-testimony requirement. Representation, duplicate recovery, lawful binding effect, and other procedural protections retain their separate law.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Reverse the categorical standing dismissal without prejudice and remand, 9–0. No back pay, benefits, or fees are awarded. Direct organizational injury is unnecessary and undecided; the unpetitioned amendment ruling is untouched, and no new declaratory claim is added. Notice to the International, alleged early layoffs, and notice exceptions remain merits issues.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** *Hunt*'s first condition and *Lujan*'s constitutional minimum remain unchanged; the third condition is displaced only within the statutory authorization. *Northeastern Florida* did not classify every *Hunt* condition as constitutional. *Rowland*'s association-capacity ruling is statutory rather than Article III law. *Matsushita* preserves actual adequacy and binding-effect protections without making possible individual issues a categorical bar to filing this action.

### Mootness and educational placement

#### Tyler Murray v. Montrose County School District RE-1J

**Docket or dockets:** Tenth Circuit Nos. 93-1466 and 93-1474.

**Decided:** December 2, 1996.

##### No live geographic placement controversy

**Holding:** The superseded placement claim is moot because the November 1993 individualized education program supplies the Olathe placement sought and no redressable residue or reasonable expectation of the same challenged transfer recurring as to Tyler is established. Recurring annual IEP reviews and an enduring disagreement about statutory meaning do not alone satisfy the exception for an injury capable of repetition yet too short for full review.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's Parts I-II, joined by Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer; eight Justices.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Court establishes no categorical rule that every replacement IEP moots every IDEA case. It decides no neighborhood-school presumption, specialized-school legality, educational adequacy, supplementary-aid exhaustion, or general additional-evidence rule.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Honig requires both same-claimant recurrence and insufficient litigation duration; Northeastern Florida's successor barrier is distinguished from the replacement IEP that removed this dispute.

##### Equitable disposition of the obsolete merits judgments

**Holding:** The Tenth Circuit's merits affirmance is vacated, and the case is remanded with instructions to vacate the district court's merits judgment on the superseded placement claim and dismiss that claim as moot. The separate cross-appeal dismissal remains undisturbed because no live additional relief is available on the obsolete placement controversy, independently of the now-vacated district-court merits victory.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's Parts I-II, joined by Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer; eight Justices.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No unrelated administrative decision is erased. No new IEP, transfer, evidence hearing or exhaustion process is commanded, and neither side receives a merits victory.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Bancorp preserves section 2106's separate equitable disposition authority and rejects automatic vacatur; its voluntary-settlement facts are absent. Munsingwear supports bounded removal of unreviewable obsolete judgments. No Rowley or Carter merits question is reached.

### Mootness, corrective jurisdiction and equitable vacatur

#### Arizonans for Official English v. Arizona

**Docket or dockets:** No. 95-974.
**Decided:** March 3, 1997.

##### Whether this former employee retains a live challenge to the employment rule

**Holding:** An individual prospective employment-rule challenge becomes moot when the employee voluntarily leaves covered employment without an intention to return, realistic recurrence or a surviving claim. A general relief request cannot manufacture nominal damages against a State or official-capacity defendant that is not a damages person under §1983.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Stevens), unanimous; all nine Justices join every holding.

**Material application:** No class or replacement claimant remained, and the dismissed individual-capacity claims were not preserved on appeal. State nonpersonhood under §1983 is a statutory bar distinct from waivable Eleventh Amendment immunity; a proposed consensual payment cannot supply the missing cause of action. The Attorney General’s constitutional participation under §2403(b) did not overturn the denial of State party status.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No general requirement to plead nominal damages by name is established. A legally available individual-capacity claim presents a different question. The Court does not fix the precise April 1990 date when mootness attached.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Preiser requires a controversy throughout review. Will and Hafer distinguish the unavailable State or official-capacity damages claim from genuine individual-capacity liability.

##### Whether limited corrective jurisdiction permits vacatur without deciding sponsor standing or constitutional merits

**Holding:** The Court may correct a lower federal court’s jurisdictional error without exercising constitutional merits jurisdiction. On the equities here, that authority requires vacating the appellate judgment and directing vacatur of the District Court declaration and dismissal for mootness, without resolving ultimate initiative-sponsor standing.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Stevens), unanimous.

**Material application:** The prevailing plaintiff ended the covered employment during review efforts; this was not settlement or abandonment by a reviewing loser. The later appellate merits adjudication and absence of a lawful damages residue justify clearing both unreviewable constitutional judgments.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Section 2106 does not independently create merits jurisdiction. Intervention, an appeal notice or political commitment does not confer automatic sponsor standing. The original declaration is not declared void when entered, and the Court resolves neither every intervention ruling nor Article XXVIII’s validity.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Vacate both merits judgments and dismiss the action as moot.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Bender and Corrick supply limited correction of jurisdictional error. Lujan and Brown Group retain constitutional injury and lawful representation requirements. Bancorp requires equitable rather than automatic vacatur; Munsingwear and Anderson support judgment clearing within that inquiry.

##### Whether state-law certification can revive a moot federal action

**Holding:** Certification of unsettled state law can assist a live federal controversy but cannot cure the absence of Article III jurisdiction. No certification is ordered in this moot case.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Stevens), unanimous.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The meaning of Article XXVIII and any constitutional challenge in future live litigation remain open. No mandatory certification requirement is adopted.

### Standing to challenge a biological consultation constraint

#### Bennett v. Spear

**Docket or dockets:** No. 95-813.
**Decided:** March 19, 1997.

##### Whether irrigators plead personal injury, traceability and redressability

**Holding:** Irrigators who allege loss of their project water from a Biological Opinion that the operating agency has announced it will follow sufficiently plead concrete injury, causation and likely redress through removal of that constraint. Standing must later be supported by the evidence appropriate to each litigation stage; a citizen-suit authorization does not replace Article III injury.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Scalia), unanimous.

**Material application:** The Bureau’s announced compliance connects the completed Service action to petitioners’ own alleged loss despite retained operational discretion. The possibility of other eventual choices does not require certainty of a changed allocation or defeat pleading-stage redress.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No biological error or final factual standing determination is made; the statutory review routes and relief remain separate.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The reviewed ESA/APA dismissal is reversed and remanded for claim-specific proceedings; the unchallenged NEPA dismissal is undisturbed.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Lujan’s personal-interest and stage-of-proof rules control, not its noncontrolling absent-agency application. Humane Society’s missing link to an already completed transfer is distinguished. Tracy permits cognizable economic injury without dispensing with other requirements, and Arizonans requires a continuing controversy without establishing a loss of this alleged injury.

For Bennett v. Spear, No. 95-813, decided March 19, 1997, see Environmental Law, “Endangered Species Act citizen-suit channels,” for the statutory channels and retained conditions, and Administrative Law, “Review of completed biological consultation,” for APA interests and finality.

### Constitutional jurisdiction and completed reporting injuries

#### Citizens for a Better Environment v. Steel Co.

**Docket or dockets:** Seventh Circuit No. 96-1136; review of 90 F.3d 1237.

**Decided:** 1997-06-02.

##### Constitutional jurisdiction before a disputed EPCRA merits question

**Holding:** A federal court cannot assume disputed Article III jurisdiction to decide the contested merits of this EPCRA reporting claim. Section 11046(c) describes enforcement and relief without making every statutory liability element jurisdictional; the holding preserves legitimate exceptional threshold sequences and does not prescribe an exhaustive order for all nonmerits issues.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's Part I, joined by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Kennedy and Thomas; five direct joins, including O'Connor and Kennedy's nonexhaustive sequencing reservation.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Whether EPCRA authorizes suit over reporting violations cured before filing remains undecided. Norton and Avrech retain their distinct settings involving already-controlled merits.

**Later-authority backlinks:** Ruhrgas AG v. Marathon Oil Co., No. 98-470, May 17, 1999: resolves the bounded nonmerits sequencing question by permitting straightforward personal-jurisdiction dismissal before a difficult subject-matter inquiry, subject to the stated state-law and statutory limits; the contested-merits prohibition remains unchanged.

##### Requested relief cannot redress this completed informational deprivation

**Holding:** Assuming past informational injury and traceability, Article III requires requested relief likely to repair that injury or prevent a supported continuing or imminent personal injury. When reports were current before suit and no continuing or imminent violation is alleged, a declaration of past lateness, Treasury penalties, monitoring, future report copies and litigation expenses supply no such redress.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's Part II, joined by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Kennedy, Thomas and Breyer; six direct joins. Stevens, Souter and Ginsburg support dismissal on statutory grounds without joining this constitutional rationale.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Court assumes rather than rejects informational injury. Standing is absent at commencement, rather than lost by later cessation. Deterrent penalties may redress a supported threatened injury; annual reporting duties alone do not allege one. Section 11046(f) permits appropriate prevailing-or-substantially-prevailing-party costs on a final order, not independent compensation. A properly presented replacement-information loss claim, monitoring in a live action, recoverable prefiling expenses, associational requirements and statutory temporal reach remain open. Section 11022(f) gives the fire department no transferable automatic inspection entitlement for this plaintiff.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Vacate and remand with directions to dismiss, unanimously as to judgment. No merits relief, standing discovery, amendment entitlement or newly inferred recurrence inquiry is ordered.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- **Bell v. Hood:** Applied to distinguish a substantial federal claim from its ultimate success; a contested statutory element is not automatically jurisdictional.
- **Young v. Northern Illinois Conference:** Applied at that same distinction; its constitutional-defense holding is not expanded into permission to assume Article III.
- **Lujan v. Defenders of Wildlife:** Its controlling standing elements and stage-appropriate support remain governing. Its three-Justice absent-agency application is neither enlarged nor needed.
- **Church of Scientology v. United States:** Meaningful partial relief remains enough; no effectual relief of that kind is available on these allegations.
- **United Food & Commercial Workers v. Brown Group:** Congress's representative-enforcement choice does not eliminate personal injury and likely redress.
- **Bennett v. Spear:** Supported pleading-stage causal and remedial links remain sufficient; its ongoing constraint differs from the completed reporting deprivation here.
- **Arizonans for Official English v. Arizona:** Applied for the live-controversy requirement and limited corrective authority to vacate without reaching merits.
- **Norton v. Mathews; Secretary of the Navy v. Avrech:** Their already-controlled merits and distinct jurisdictional settings remain intact; neither decides this unresolved EPCRA dispute.
- **Gwaltney of Smithfield v. Chesapeake Bay Foundation:** Its different statutory wording and enforcement scheme do not settle the merits question the Court leaves open.
- **Meghrig v. KFC Western:** Its RCRA cause-and-remedy limits remain statute-specific; it supplies no automatic EPCRA current-violation rule.

### Mootness and conditional reenactment

#### Timmons v. Twin Cities Area New Party

**Docket or dockets:** No. 95-1608.
**Decided:** 1997-04-28.

For the separate questions in Timmons v. Twin Cities Area New Party, 1997-04-28, see Election Law — Ballot access and political parties.

##### The conditional enactment leaves a live controversy

**Holding:** A challenge to the old prohibition remains live where the officials seek reversal of its invalidation and an enacted provision expressly revives that prohibition upon reversal. Chapter 419 supplies that concrete consequence; neither its replacement scheme nor completion of the 1994 election makes this dispute advisory.

**Proposition-level authority:** Thomas's Parts I and III, joined by all seven other Associates; eight direct joins. Stone-Zsela agrees to reach the merits without an additional statutory rationale.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No stay, mandate recall or compliance with the replacement scheme is assumed; its independent constitutionality and the governing law of intervening filings remain open.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** City of Mesquite prevents automatic mootness where repeal leaves recurrence consequential; Norman preserves a continuing election controversy after the immediate election.

### Pre-enforcement injury and ripeness

#### Pic-A-State Pennsylvania, Inc. v. Reno

**Docket or dockets:** Third Circuit No. 95-7137; no separate Supreme Court docket assigned.
**Decided:** 1997-04-28.

For the separate questions in Pic-A-State Pennsylvania, Inc. v. Reno, 1997-04-28, see Constitutional Structure — Interstate lottery procurement.

##### The stopped business presents a concrete and ripe challenge

**Holding:** A business presents a concrete, ripe pre-enforcement challenge when a criminal statute directly reaches its existing activity, it has stopped that activity but intends to resume, serious penalties create a sufficiently real enforcement risk, and the requested relief would remove that present federal constraint. Those conditions are met here; neither an actual prosecution nor resolution of every independent state-law restriction is required.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Kennedy), unanimous.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Government's prediction of unlikely prosecution is not a disavowal; lack of disavowal alone is not sufficient in every case. Independent state restrictions need not all disappear for federal relief to redress the federal barrier.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Abbott Laboratories supplies fitness and hardship; Bennett requires personal injury, causation and likely redress at the relevant stage. Younger supplies no abstention ground without a pending state prosecution.

#### Lee v. Harcleroad

**Docket or dockets:** Ninth Circuit Nos. 95-35804, 95-35805, 95-35854, 95-35948 and 95-35949; no separate Supreme Court docket stated.

**Decided:** 1998-01-20.

##### Patient exposure and the distinct reduction-of-care theory

**Holding:** At summary judgment, a plaintiff seeking prospective relief must support a concrete, actual or imminent personal injury with specific facts by affidavit or other evidence, allowing favorable reasonable inferences. Elsner's progressive illness and prior depression, even with statutory eligibility and the general medical assertions assumed, do not establish sufficiently impending exposure to an unwanted assisted death; likewise, an assumed reduction of medical-care protection does not establish injury without supported personal exposure to changed care or threatened harm from that reduction.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stevens's Part I-A has all nine Justices. Part I-B, addressing the separate care-protection theory, has all eight Associate Justices; Stone-Zsela agrees in that disposition only.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The summary-judgment rule requires neither certainty nor a numerical probability, particular document or proof that safeguards never fail. The construction of §4.01(1), retained negligence and misconduct liability under §4.02, substantive safeguards, patient-claim traceability and redressability remain undecided.

##### Continuing controversy, representation and the proposed class

**Holding:** Dutson's personal prospective controversy does not continue after his death on this record, which establishes no surviving personal claim, substitute or certified class. A provider's relationship to patients and a proposed class never certified cannot supply the represented patients' missing injury or an independently continuing controversy.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stevens's Part II, joined by all seven other Associate Justices, controls with eight votes; Stone-Zsela agrees in these dispositions only.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No categorical ban on provider representation, universal class relation-back rule or damages-pleading condition is adopted.

##### Compelled participation: injury and ripeness

**Holding:** Absence of an express criminal or statutory penalty does not by itself defeat Article III injury: an actually applicable legal obligation or supported threatened civil enforcement may burden present conduct. Fitness and hardship must both be examined; predominantly legal questions do not alone establish hardship, but the possibility of a later civil-enforcement suit does not categorically require postponing every challenge to a presently burdensome legal duty. The participation claims must be reconsidered under these principles without a present finding of standing or ripeness.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stevens's Part III-A states the legal boundary with all eight Associate Justices joining. Part III-B's application and remand command six direct votes: Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer. Stone-Zsela, Scalia and Thomas oppose the remand; no combination of separate rationales is needed for controlling authority.

**Material application:** Further analysis must distinguish the express freedom to refuse participation under §4.01(4) from its transfer-on-request duty upon care transfer, the attending-physician setting in §3.01(2), and the long-term-care witness rule in §2.02(4). Actual duty, present burden, proper defendant, traceability, redressability, fitness and hardship must be established; no duty to prescribe or recommend assistance is found.

##### Facility association

**Holding:** The facilities' challenge to §4.01(2)'s alleged restriction on excluding participating physicians lacks actual or imminent injury where no intending participating physician at the facilities or present operational restriction is supported. That separate dismissal remains operative despite remand of the compelled-participation claims.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stevens's Part IV has all nine Justices.

**Limits and questions not reached:** An actual physician-participation conflict or supported present burden would present a different case. Association and religious-freedom merits and conclusive statutory construction remain open.

##### The District Court's declaration and injunction remain vacated

**Holding:** Limited corrective jurisdiction permits clearing a constitutional merits judgment unsupported by a live injured claimant, and the equities here require leaving vacated the District Court's patient-based equal-protection declaration and permanent injunction. Reconsideration of a distinct, previously undecided provider claim does not restore that judgment or establish a new entitlement to relief.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stevens's Part V has all eight Associate Justices; Stone-Zsela agrees in leaving the judgment and injunction vacated only.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Vacatur is equitable, not automatic whenever jurisdiction is disputed; §2106 supplies corrective authority, not merits jurisdiction.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Affirm patient, reduction-of-care, representation/class and facility-association dismissals without prejudice; preserve vacatur of the patient-based declaration and injunction. Vacate the participation dismissal for claim-specific injury and ripeness analysis, 6–3. No assisted-death merits, restored injunction or automatic hearing is ordered.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Lujan is applied for specific summary-judgment support, reasonable favorable inferences and the concrete-interest requirement. Its ordinary injury, traceability and redressability elements remain intact; its noncontrolling absent-agency application is not adopted.
- Lyons and Whitmore retain the requirement of supported actual or imminent personal exposure; severity of a possible harm does not replace that connection.
- Powers is applied without combining its own-stake, close-relationship and hindrance requirements or creating a universal bar to provider representation.
- Arizonans is applied for continuing controversy and limited jurisdictional correction. Bender and Corrick retain their corrective role; Bancorp retains equitable vacatur, and Munsingwear its role in clearing judgments in the proper circumstances.
- Abbott Laboratories is applied to both fitness and hardship; Toilet Goods is confined to its actual enforcement and review setting. Babbitt supplies protection against requiring completed prosecution, without establishing that these conditional duties actually apply.
- Vacco and Glucksberg remain decisions permitting their specified prohibitions. Neither requires prohibition, validates Oregon's safeguards or supplies opponents' standing.
- Tanford's actual continuing exposure, Jeffries's live-relief boundary, Chicago's distinction between jurisdiction and merits, and Rouse's supported-threat requirement remain intact; none decides this statute's duties.

### Standing and intervention in school-religion litigation

#### Ingebretsen v. Jackson Public School District

**Docket or dockets:** Fifth Circuit No. 94-60631; 88 F.3d 274 identifies the judgment below.
**Decided:** March 19, 1997.

For the official-school-worship grounds and limits on the interim injunction, see *Ingebretsen*, March 19, 1997, No. 94-60631 (First Amendment — Official school worship and private student expression).

##### Whether imminent personal school-event exposure supports prospective standing

**Holding:** Students concretely and imminently facing an official school-event mechanism may challenge that threatened personal exposure before it occurs, where the injury is causally connected to the mechanism and prospectively redressable. Standing does not establish that the threatened conduct is unlawful.

**Proposition-level authority:** Ginsburg's Opinion of the Court, Part I, unanimous.

**Material application:** The enrolled students face impending events under a mandatory permission mechanism administered by officials, supported by the hearing record. Lack of a completed local policy does not make that prospect remote; parents may represent their children's interests through the proper representative route.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No general taxpayer standing or standing for an abstract objector elsewhere is recognized.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** *Lujan* supplies concrete injury, causation, and redressability; standing is not derived from abstract opposition to a statute.

##### Whether the student applicants established inadequate representation under Rule 24(a)(2)

**Holding:** A timely applicant with a protectable interest that litigation may impair is entitled to intervene when existing representation may be inadequate. Shared desired results and the State's official identity do not conclusively prove adequacy, but the Attorney General actually represented these applicants' private-expression interests and no relevant omitted interest was identified; denial stands without reaching unnecessary alternative elements.

**Proposition-level authority:** Ginsburg's Opinion of the Court, Part V-A, joined by all eight Associate Justices; Stone-Zsela dissents from this component.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Amicus participation cannot substitute for an otherwise established intervention right. Materially changed interests may support a proper later application, without an advance entitlement or a required new adequacy hearing.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** *Trbovich* supplies the minimal burden of possible inadequacy and recognizes potentially different governmental and private interests; the inquiry remains individualized.

##### Whether a common question compels permissive intervention

**Holding:** Rule 24(b)'s common-question condition permits intervention but does not require it. The court retains discretion considering undue delay or prejudice, and its duplication-and-delay assessment justifies denial in this narrow statutory proceeding.

**Proposition-level authority:** Ginsburg's Opinion of the Court, Part V-B, joined by all eight Associate Justices; Stone-Zsela agrees with the disposition only.

**Material application:** Available amicus submissions support the conclusion that excluding duplicative party participation caused no demonstrated prejudice; they do not defeat intervention as of right.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Court creates no novelty-of-argument requirement, unreviewable discretion, or universal plain-error prerequisite.

### Electoral informational injury and statutory review

#### Akins v. FEC

**Docket or dockets:** D.C. Circuit No. 94-5088; no separate Supreme Court docket supplied.

**Decided:** 1997-06-25.

For political-committee coverage and the separate membership and transaction provisions, see Akins v. FEC, D.C. Circuit No. 94-5088, decided 1997-06-25, Election Law — Political-committee coverage and membership communications.

##### I. The continuing denial of particular electoral information is a concrete injury

**Holding:** Voters denied particular campaign-financing information allegedly required by FECA, which they seek for their own electoral participation, have a concrete informational injury even when many voters share it. Here the Commission's legal ground blocks a statutory process capable of supplying information still sought; the possibility of a later lawful discretionary choice does not defeat traceability or redressability.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part I of Souter's Court opinion is joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Ginsburg and Breyer: seven direct votes.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Statutory complainant status, opposition to an organization's policies or a generalized wish for legal compliance alone is insufficient. The plaintiffs must support their own injury at the litigation stage; standing determines neither entitlement to every requested item nor ultimate regulatory classification.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Public Citizen supports a concrete widely shared informational injury. Lujan retains personal injury and stage-appropriate proof. Bennett requires examination of the real remedial chain despite lawful discretion. Steel Co.'s completed deprivation and already-current reports are distinguished from information still withheld.

##### II. FECA permits review of this dismissal's legal premise

**Holding:** Section 437g(a)(8) permits an aggrieved complainant to obtain judicial review of a complaint dismissal within sixty days and permits correction of a legally erroneous coverage premise. A court may declare the dismissal contrary to law and may direct conformity within thirty days; if the Commission fails to conform, the complainant may bring an action to remedy the violation involved in the original complaint.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part II has the same seven-Justice coalition as Part I.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The separate 120-day inaction route is not invoked. Judicial correction does not compel prosecution, disclosure or penalties, remove lawful discretion, or permit substitution of the agency's separate corporate-prohibition membership rationale for the actual political-committee dismissal ground. Genuine ambiguity and remaining discretion remain for lawful agency consideration.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Standing and legal-error review are affirmed, 7–2; the case returns through the enacted review channel. The separate corporate-prohibition nonenforcement choice is not disturbed merely by correcting this dismissal.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Brown Group separates statutory enforcement authority from Article III. Bennett requires an actual authorized channel, and Legi-Tech preserves lawful agency reconsideration and statutory prerequisites. The agency's interpretation of constitutional decisions is not conclusive because it administers FECA.

### Legislator standing

#### Raines v. Byrd

**Docket or dockets:** No. 96-1671.

**Decided:** June 26, 1997.

##### Whether individual Members may challenge abstract institutional dilution after fully effective votes

**Holding:** Individual Members of Congress whose votes were counted and given full effect lack Article III standing to challenge the Line Item Veto Act on the asserted abstract dilution of Congress's institutional power and alteration of future votes. Express statutory permission to sue removes the applicable prudential obstacle but cannot supply the missing concrete personal injury.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Kennedy, Thomas and Ginsburg; six direct rationale votes. Souter supplies the seventh judgment vote on a separate ground.

**Material application:** The Members lost votes that were counted and identify no personal injury from a cancellation; neither House authorized them to represent Congress.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No categorical bar to legislator or official injury is established. Personal exclusion, lost salary, nullified decisive votes, an authorized institutional claim satisfying Article III and concrete cancellation injury remain distinct. Legislative remedies create no exhaustion requirement; no independent traceability holding is added.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Lujan supplies concrete personal injury, without enlargement of its noncontrolling redressability analysis. Akins's concrete shared informational injury is distinguished. Powell preserves personal exclusion claims; Coleman preserves complete nullification of decisive votes. Bender and Allen support the personal-stake and unauthorized-representation boundaries.

##### What disposition follows from the Members' lack of standing

**Holding:** Because these plaintiffs lack Article III standing, the District Court's constitutional declaration must be vacated and the complaint dismissed for lack of jurisdiction. The Court does not decide the Act's constitutionality, nondelegation, Presentment or the independent ripeness question.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Kennedy, Thomas and Ginsburg; six direct rationale votes. Souter supplies the seventh judgment vote on a separate ground.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Vacate the District Court's constitutional declaration and remand for jurisdictional dismissal, 7–2. Vacatur neither sustains the Act nor orders a merits remand.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Steel Co. requires resolution of disputed Article III power before disputed merits in this setting; statutory expedition and direct review do not supply constitutional injury.

### Official injury and statutory commands

#### Printz v. United States; Mack v. United States

**Docket or dockets:** Nos. 95-1478 and 95-1503.

**Decided:** June 27, 1997.

**Companion or consolidated matters:** The consolidated Printz and Mack dockets receive the same confined constitutional disposition.

For the limited executive-assistance rule and the fractured fiscal mandate in Printz/Mack, June 27, 1997, Nos. 95-1478 and 95-1503, see Constitutional Structure — Temporary state executive assistance.

##### Whether the plaintiff officers may challenge duties imposed on their offices

**Holding:** A federal command requiring these plaintiff officers to perform identified work through their offices presents a concrete injury redressable by relief against that command. Their official status does not bar review, but it supplies no standing to obtain an advisory decision about independent obligations imposed only on absent dealers or purchasers.

**Proposition-level authority:** Jurisdictional section of Souter's opinion, unanimous.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Government's disavowal of criminal prosecution does not remove the compulsory civil work. Separate criminal-vagueness, unraised civil-vagueness and Thirteenth Amendment matters outside the grant remain undisturbed.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Raines's unauthorized institutional dilution is distinguished from duties imposed directly on the challengers; no universal bar to official injury or standing for absent parties follows.

### Standing and controlled merits

#### Alexander v. Whitman

**Docket or dockets:** Third Circuit No. 95-5414; no separate Supreme Court docket stated.

**Decided:** 1998-02-02.

For personhood, maternal liberty and the separate tort-remedy equality rules and the case-wide mandate, see Alexander v. Whitman, Third Circuit No. 95-5414; no separate Supreme Court docket stated, decided 1998-02-02, Civil Rights — Stillbirth, personhood and state tort remedies.

##### The actual denied-letter controversy permits bounded review

**Holding:** At the pleading stage, Alexander's alleged actual denial of letters needed for her identified state action supplies a concrete personal obstacle traceable to the responsible surrogate and capable of meaningful partial redress. That controversy permits review of her own constitutional challenge; the exact child-personhood question is already controlled by Roe and falls within the confined controlled-merits setting preserved in Steel Co., without a finding that every proposed estate or class capacity exists.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part I of Souter's opinion has all eight Associate Justices. Stone-Zsela agrees in reaching the authorized claims without joining that threshold rationale.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The mother’s own denied-letter injury supports her challenge; no general weak-merits exception to constitutional jurisdiction, blanket estate capacity, lawyer standing or class status is recognized.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Lujan, Lee and Yankton require a concrete personal obstacle, stage-appropriate support and meaningful redress. Steel Co. preserves the confined Norton/Avrech already-controlled settings without permitting assumed constitutional power over contested merits. No independent lawyer or class jurisdiction is adjudicated.

### Privacy Act standing and jurisdictional dismissal

#### J. Roderick MacArthur Foundation and Lance E. Lindblom v. Federal Bureau of Investigation

**Docket or dockets:** D.C. Circuit No. 95-5386; no separate Supreme Court docket stated.

**Decided:** 1998-02-13.

For continued-retention duties, adverse effect and statutory remedies and the case-wide mandate, see J. Roderick MacArthur Foundation and Lance E. Lindblom v. Federal Bureau of Investigation, D.C. Circuit No. 95-5386; no separate Supreme Court docket stated, decided 1998-02-13, Administrative Law — Privacy Act retention and continuing pertinence.

##### A live personal statutory controversy and an express statutory action permit merits review

**Holding:** Actual continued custody of identified records describing this claimant's protected associations supplies a concrete personal information interest directly traceable to the retaining agency; an actually requested award within §552a(g)(1)(D)/(g)(4)'s legally available monetary category can meaningfully redress the claimed invasion if its requirements are established. Jurisdiction does not depend on first proving the disputed retention violation or final damages entitlement, and the substantial statutory claim falls within the Act's express agency action and conditional sovereign consent without creating a new constitutional damages cause.

**Proposition-level authority:** Breyer's opinion: Part I, joined by all eight Associate Justices; Stone-Zsela agrees in reaching the merits without joining this rationale.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Act’s express agency action and conditional monetary consent supply the route; §1331 is not a waiver, §702 supplies no money and no agency Bivens cause is created. Actual monetary relief was requested, but culpability, adverse effect and entitlement remain separate.

##### The separate constitutional claims lack the required supported connection

**Holding:** The Foundation's affidavits do not establish the causal mechanism connecting FBI retention to prospective grantees' or employers' knowledge and reactions; Lindblom's separately presented constitutional claim supplies no independent supported basis beyond that failed derivative theory. Both constitutional-standing rejections therefore stand, without a ruling on the First Amendment merits or a categorical exclusion of reputational or associational injury.

**Proposition-level authority:** Breyer's opinion: Part IV-A has all nine Justices. Scalia and Thomas's additional injury/imminence ground is separate and noncontrolling.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Meese’s supported public-label mechanism is absent. No categorical bar on reputational or associational injury is adopted; the actual personal statutory custody claim is distinct from subjective constitutional chill or the unsupported audience-exposure mechanism.

##### The constitutional dismissals must be without prejudice

**Holding:** A dismissal of these constitutional claims for want of Article III standing cannot carry a merits-preclusive effect. The standing rejections are sustained, but the district judgment's with-prejudice effect is vacated and the case is remanded to conform those dismissals to their jurisdictional ground.

**Proposition-level authority:** Breyer's opinion: Part IV-B has all eight Associates; Stone-Zsela agrees in the disposition only. The correction is unanimous.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Steel Co. and Bell v. Hood distinguish constitutional jurisdiction from disputed statutory merits and ultimate recovery. Lujan, Warth and Havens require a concrete personal interest with stage-appropriate proof; Meyer requires a separate authorized action and sovereign consent. Laird and Meese preserve the distinction between subjective chill and a supported mechanism for reputational harm. Arizonans and Lee permit corrective jurisdiction and require the constitutional dismissals to remain without merits prejudice; Alexander preserves meaningful partial redress without relaxing the procedural-stage requirement.

### Competitor standing and statutory review

#### National Credit Union Administration v. First National Bank & Trust Co.; AT&T Family Federal Credit Union v. First National Bank & Trust Co.

**Docket or dockets:** Nos. 96-843 and 96-847.

**Decided:** 1998-02-25.

##### These banks may seek review of the membership expansion

**Holding:** Directly competing banks injured by these completed charter approvals have Article III standing and fall at least arguably within the interests implicated by §109’s particular membership boundary for APA §702 review. They need not be the statute’s specially intended beneficiaries, but competitive injury alone does not establish a right to challenge every regulatory limitation.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Stone-Zsela), joined by Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas and Ginsburg; five direct joins.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The banks’ traceable, redressable competitive injury and direct connection to the particular customer-market boundary satisfy distinct constitutional and statutory inquiries. This does not establish that protecting bank profits was the statute’s purpose, abolish zone-of-interests screening, or excuse APA finality, adequate-remedy, preclusion or other review conditions.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Lujan preserves injury, traceability and redressability as separate constitutional requirements.
- Bennett is applied to the interests arguably protected by the particular provision and preserves APA review conditions.
- Clarke and Data Processing supply the permissive, provision-connected competitor inquiry without a special-beneficiary requirement.
- Air Courier preserves the distinction between injury and the statutory zone; its derivative employment interest is distinguished.

For this case’s other questions, see Business and Commercial Law (Unrelated occupational groups cannot replace the common bond; The disposition enforces the challenged boundary without rewriting membership law).

### Mootness and continuing territorial declarations

#### South Dakota v. Yankton Sioux Tribe

**Docket or dockets:** No. 96-1581.

**Decided:** 1998-01-26.

For reservation diminishment and the parcel-specific territorial remedy and the case-wide mandate, see South Dakota v. Yankton Sioux Tribe, No. 96-1581, decided 1998-01-26, Federal Indian Law — Reservation diminishment.

##### The existing declaration presents a continuing concrete controversy

**Holding:** The EPA's waiver of a particular liner requirement does not moot the State's challenge where the operative declaration continues to determine the territorial legal regime governing its issued permit for this identified site. That continuing legal relationship permits review; anticipated similar projects or a wish to erase unfavorable precedent alone would not.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Stevens), unanimous.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Lee requires a concrete continuing legal relationship and distinguishes corrective jurisdiction from merits power. An operative territorial declaration affecting the issued permit remains consequential after the particular EPA liner waiver; unfavorable precedent or hoped-for future projects alone would not suffice.

### Ripeness and emergency notification

#### W.P. v. Verniero

**Docket or dockets:** Third Circuit No. 96-5416; the distinct E.B. appeal, No. 96-5132, is outside review.

**Decided:** 1998-03-16.

##### The advance-notice emergency question remains unripe

**Holding:** The present class challenge does not decide whether a concrete emergency may justify notification without ordinary advance notice because no representative alleges such notification and no prosecutor is shown to have done or planned it. The ordinary-process injunction is retained subject to that express reservation; it neither approves every emergency bypass nor prohibits every judicially authorized exception.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part IV of Souter's opinion, joined by all seven other Associates. Stone-Zsela agrees with the disposition of the separately reserved emergency component, adding no emergency rationale or new exception.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The described mechanism permits seeking a judicial order when Tier 3 release information arrives after release or the registrant evades service despite good-faith efforts; after three days without completed service the prosecutor may apply. Permission to apply is not an obligation or automatic exception to notice. No actual or planned use affecting a representative is established.

For this case’s other questions, see Criminal Procedure (The challenged notification is not punishment on this record); Civil Rights (The State must justify both tier and notification plan before ordinary notification; Risk classification requires clear and convincing proof; the plan injunction is retained on a narrower authority basis).

### Ripeness of proposed preclearance exemptions

#### Texas v. United States

**Docket or dockets:** No. 97-29.

**Decided:** 1998-03-31.

##### A categorical preclearance exemption is unripe before the challenged intervention is sufficiently defined

**Holding:** A request to exempt possible future school-district interventions from §5 is unripe when no sufficiently definite intervention, affected district or allocation of powers establishes a fit legal controversy and no substantial present hardship justifies deciding before those details exist. Texas's challenge therefore must be dismissed without deciding whether any particular intervention changes voting or whether a supported federal-funding conflict affects that question.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stevens's Opinion of the Court, joined by all eight other Justices: nine direct joins.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No particular school-district intervention, voting change or federal-funding conflict is decided. The rule creates neither a categorical school-intervention exemption nor a requirement to violate the law before challenging a concrete obligation.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The ripeness dismissal is affirmed unanimously; sufficiently definite implementation and an actual controversy may support later review through the proper channel.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- **Abbott Laboratories v. Gardner:** Applied at the fitness-and-hardship inquiry; uncertainty alone does not require an advisory declaration.
- **New York v. United States:** Distinguished because its future command already required substantial present siting action; timely pre-enforcement review remains available in that setting.
- **Pic-A-State Pennsylvania v. Reno:** Distinguished because an actual criminal constraint had already stopped the plaintiff's existing business; no prosecution prerequisite is added.
- **Presley v. Etowah County Commission / Mack v. Russell County Commission:** Preserved at its direct voting-relation rule and reservation concerning effective replacement of elected authority; no future intervention is classified here.
- **Steel Co. v. Citizens for a Better Environment:** Applied to require the Article III inquiry before contested statutory merits.
- **Raines v. Byrd:** Its concrete-injury discipline is respected without extending its individual-legislator rule into a categorical bar on governmental plaintiffs.

### Continuing controversy over supervised-release conditions

#### United States v. Bird

**Docket or dockets:** No. 97-1270; Fifth Circuit No. 95-20792.

**Decided:** 1998-04-13.

##### The supervision component requires a determination of a continuing controversy before its merits

**Holding:** When the actual operation of a challenged supervision condition and any continuing redressable consequence remain unresolved, a live challenge to the conviction does not itself establish jurisdiction to decide that condition's merits. The appellate disposition affirming the condition is vacated, and the matter returns through the Fifth Circuit to determine that threshold on the actual supervision record; the district condition itself is neither vacated nor rewritten by this decision.

**Proposition-level authority:** Kennedy's Part II-A, joined by all eight other Justices, controls the continuing-controversy requirement and the limited appellate vacatur. Part II-B's precise remand and reservation of the condition's merits is joined by Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer: eight Justices including the author. Stone-Zsela does not join Part II-B and would add mandatory replacement if the dispute is live.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The actual supervision record must establish operation and redressable consequences; dates and announced terms do not establish commencement, tolling, expiration or a present burden. If live, §3583(d) requires reasonable relation to sentencing purposes, no greater liberty deprivation than reasonably necessary and compliance with pertinent policy statements. If moot, merits cannot be decided; disposition and equitable treatment depend on actual circumstances.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The appellate disposition affirming the 1,000-foot condition is vacated unanimously. Eight Associates adopt the jurisdictional remand without deciding the condition’s merits; the district condition is not vacated or rewritten. No present requirement to retain, narrow or remove it, general resentencing or extension follows.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Spencer v. Kemna, decided March 3, 1998, supplies the actual-continuing-injury principle. Its revocation holding is not enlarged into a categorical rule about every direct sentencing challenge or a finding that this condition has expired.
- Madsen v. Women's Health Center, Inc., 512 U.S. 753 (1994), and Schenck v. Pro-Choice Network, decided February 19, 1997, remain relevant fit analogies if condition merits become reachable. Their injunction rules establish neither an automatic criminal-supervision test nor a universal permissible distance here.

### Standing to contest grand-jury discrimination

#### Campbell v. Louisiana

**Docket or dockets:** No. 96-1584.

**Decided:** 1998-04-21.

##### Campbell may assert the excluded jurors' equal-protection rights

**Holding:** A white defendant may challenge purposeful racial exclusion of Black citizens in the judicial selection of a voting member of the grand jury indicting him when the defendant suffers concrete injury, has the required relationship with the excluded citizens and they face substantial obstacles to asserting their own rights. Louisiana's selection of the foreperson from the venire before constituting the remainder of the grand jury satisfies those standing conditions; discrimination itself remains to be proved.

**Proposition-level authority:** Breyer's Part I, joined by Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter and Ginsburg, has six direct votes and independently controls this route.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The judge selects a full voting foreperson from the venire before the remaining members are drawn. Standing establishes neither purpose nor a right to proportional representation.

##### Campbell may litigate his own due-process objection

**Holding:** A defendant has standing to litigate his own due-process objection when alleged racial discrimination affected the composition of the grand jury exercising charging authority over him; his different race does not eliminate that personal injury. Louisiana's method selects a voting member of the accusatory body, so Hobby does not foreclose Campbell's claim.

**Proposition-level authority:** Breyer's Part II is joined by all eight other Justices and supplies an independently sufficient unanimous standing ground.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The nature and extent of the underlying due-process entitlement, violation and harmlessness remain open. The historical absence of Black forepersons is not itself a Supreme Court finding of discriminatory purpose.

##### The fair-cross-section theory is not reached, and relief remains conditional

**Holding:** The Court does not reach Campbell's Sixth Amendment fair-cross-section theory because proper presentation to the Louisiana appellate courts has not been shown. Reversal of the standing judgment permits consideration of the recognized discrimination claims under applicable proof and remedial rules; it does not itself establish entitlement to a new trial or invalidate the indictment.

**Proposition-level authority:** Breyer's Part III has all nine Justices' joins as to preservation and the common bounded remand; Part I's six-Justice coalition supplies the additional equal-protection route on remand.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The standing judgment is reversed and remanded unanimously, with six Justices additionally adopting the third-party route. Proof, preserved defenses and remedy remain for lawful further proceedings; neither indictment invalidation nor an unconditional new trial follows.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Powers supplies the concrete-injury, relationship and enforcement-obstacle requirements, applied here to selection of a voting grand-jury member.
- McCollum reinforces juror equality and the ordinary third-party-standing requirements; its distinct private-defense attribution holding is not enlarged by this direct judicial selection case.
- J.E.B. supports individual juror equality and the litigant's challenge, without proportional representation or presumed group loyalties; its sex-strike merits and proof rules remain distinct.
- Hobby is distinguished by selection of an officer from an already constituted grand jury; its ministerial-office analysis does not resolve discriminatory selection of a voting member.
- Adams v. Robertson supports the requirement to establish proper presentation before reaching the unaddressed federal claim.

### Third-party standing in citizenship transmission

#### Miller v. Albright

**Docket or dockets:** No. 96-1060.

**Decided:** 1998-04-22.

##### Miller may present the father's equality claim

**Holding:** In this citizenship-transmission challenge, Miller may assert her citizen father's equality claim under the existing third-party-standing precedents despite his dismissal and failure to pursue an appeal, while her own claimed citizenship remains at stake. This permission does not establish constitutional invalidity, entitlement to citizenship or a general right to litigate every parent's constitutional interests.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stevens's express permission, Scalia's concurrence joined by Thomas, and Ginsburg's dissent joined by Souter and Breyer supply six direct positions at this precise scope. They do not share a complete merits or remedial rationale; no combination under Marks is needed for their express agreement on this separate proposition.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The six agreeing Justices do not establish a general parent-child exemption from the separate impediment inquiry. The daughter’s independent extraterritorial rights, scrutiny, constitutional validity and citizenship remedy remain outside this shared proposition. See this case under Immigration for the fractured affirmance.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Craig supplies the six-Justice permission at the stated scope; it creates no common merits or citizenship-remedy ground.
- Powers and Campbell retain distinct injury, relationship and enforcement-obstacle requirements; no majority here announces a general exemption for parent-child litigation.
- Lujan's distinct Article III requirements are not displaced by the judgment or the case-specific third-party permission.

### Ripeness of substantive forest-plan challenges

#### Ohio Forestry Association, Inc. v. Sierra Club

**Docket or dockets:** No. 97-16.
**Decided:** 1998-05-18.

##### The present substantive planning challenge is premature

**Holding:** A substantive challenge to a programmatic forest plan is unripe when the plan authorizes no immediate logging, establishes no sufficient present legal hardship, and leaves further project decisions and meaningful review before cutting. On this record that rule requires dismissal without prejudice of the plan/clearcutting, public-trust sales and timber-suitability-regulation claims, without deciding their substantive validity.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's opinion of the Court, joined in full by all nine Justices, supplies direct controlling authority for this rule and the limits below.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Court decides neither standing, APA finality, statutory or public-trust merits, nor an independent fee entitlement. It does not adopt the lower court's exceptional-circumstances formulation for even-aged management or equate an optimum-method inquiry with a resource-protection condition. A site-specific authorization, an immediately binding provision or regulation, or a properly presented completed procedural violation may receive review when its independent requirements are satisfied. Lujan's protection of a procedural right tied to a threatened concrete interest remains intact. No optional-appeal exhaustion requirement or future limitations ruling is adopted.

Assertions in later briefing concerning motorcycles, heavy machinery, road closures and hiking trails supply no established basis for resolving the reviewed substantive dispute differently. The Court does not find those activities or injuries proved, find intentional waiver, announce a general forfeiture rule, or adjudicate a distinct present-recreation claim on either ripeness or merits.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Both lower merits adjudications are displaced, and all three substantive theories are dismissed without prejudice as unripe. No plan approval or invalidation, logging injunction, revision or fee award follows. Later review must satisfy its own requirements and need not await physical injury.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Abbott Laboratories v. Gardner and Texas v. United States: applied to fitness and present hardship; neither requires an advisory answer about an insufficiently concrete implementation.
- Toilet Goods Association v. Gardner: applied to distinguish completed agency action from ripeness for judicial review.
- Bennett v. Spear: distinguished at its supported present-consequences premise; later implementation discretion alone does not defeat review.
