- New York v. United States and Pic-A-State Pennsylvania v. Reno: distinguished because their challenged rules imposed established present burdens; pre-enforcement review remains available on that basis.
- Lujan v. Defenders of Wildlife: its concrete-interest procedural protection is preserved; this decision supplies no separate standing determination.

### Association standing to challenge unequal bidding opportunity

#### Engineering Contractors Association of South Florida v. Metropolitan Dade County

**Docket or dockets:** 97-1115 and 97-1160.

**Decided:** 1998-09-21.

##### Unequal competitive opportunity supports this review

**Holding:** Regular bidders facing a supported likelihood of exclusion from equal competition have an injury even without proving that they would win a contract. The associations may seek common prospective relief where their members possess standing, the interest is germane, and the claims and relief do not require individual participation; facts may be stipulated, but jurisdiction may not.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (O'Connor). Part I of O'Connor's opinion has all eight Associates. Stone-Zsela joins the equal-competition injury proposition and the distinction between subsidiary factual review and constitutional legal review; he does not add a distinct associational-standing rationale.

**Material application:** Northeastern Florida Chapter, Associated General Contractors v. Jacksonville identifies the denial of equal bidding opportunity as the injury. United Food & Commercial Workers Union Local 751 v. Brown Group preserves the association requirements and their distinct constitutional and prudential roles. The associations' regularly bidding members and stipulated likely future exclusion establish the necessary injury for all three programs, and the common justification challenge permits common prospective relief. No lost award or injury from every hypothetical contract mechanism is required. This does not confer standing to litigate every distinct operational challenge. Under §1254(1), the Court reviews the final appellate judgment. Subsidiary historical findings receive clear-error review; constitutional sufficiency, the level of scrutiny and remedial fit require independent legal assessment.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Northeastern Florida applies unequal-competition injury; its standing rule does not decide the preferences' validity.
- Brown Group applies the association requirements at their stated scope; its standing rule does not decide the preferences' validity.

Related questions in Engineering Contractors Association of South Florida v. Metropolitan Dade County, 1998-09-21, dockets 97-1115 and 97-1160, appear under Civil Rights — Local contracting preferences and separate racial and sex scrutiny.

### Continuing controversy over recurring congressional nomination rules

#### Libertarian Party of Illinois v. Rednour

**Docket or dockets:** 97-25.

**Decided:** 1998-09-21.

##### The recurring access controversy remains reviewable

**Holding:** Completion of the 1996 primary does not end this recurring challenge to continuing party classifications and nomination requirements. The Court reaches the access questions without reopening that primary or adopting apparent petition insufficiency as an independent standing ground.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Souter). Souter's Part I has all eight Associates; Stone-Zsela also joins the recurring-controversy and no-reopened-election conclusions, without an additional petition-sufficiency rationale.

**Material application:** Norman v. Reed preserves review of recurring election conditions that otherwise escape decision within a completed election cycle. These classifications continue to govern the party's congressional route, so the passage of the particular primary does not resolve the dispute. The Court does not independently adjudicate standing on a new assessment of the rejected sheets, or convert acceptance of other deficient petitions into proof of legal compliance. Review permits deciding the continuing rule; it does not require undoing a completed election.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Norman applies at its election-continuity scope without ordering a new election.

Related questions in Libertarian Party of Illinois v. Rednour, 1998-09-21, dockets 97-25, appear under Election Law — Congressional nomination routes and relevant-electorate support; Constitutional Structure — Genuine nomination procedures and congressional qualifications.

### Direct review and concrete cancellation injuries

#### Clinton v. City of New York

**Docket or dockets:** No. 97-1374; consolidated New York and Snake River actions.
**Decided:** 1998-06-25.

For the other questions decided in this same case, docket and date, see Constitutional Structure (questions: The President may implement enacted choices but may not cancel their legal force on a new policy judgment).

##### Section 692 permits these organizational challengers' direct review

**Holding:** In §692's particular expedited constitutional-review scheme, an adversely affected individual includes a separately injured juridical claimant such as the organizational plaintiffs here. That statutory permission supplies this direct-review channel but does not dispense with Article III standing or the statute's separate procedural requirements.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Stevens). Stevens, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg directly join the statutory-channel part. Stone-Zsela and Breyer accept review without joining its complete rationale.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The construction is specific to this expedited scheme. Prompt complaint delivery to both Houses, their intervention rights and the House's intervention without a special resolution, the ten-calendar-day appeal and thirty-day jurisdictional-statement deadlines, the single-Justice-stay prohibition and expedition remain operative; no disputed filing compliance is decided. Ordinary federal-question review remains available, and alternative certiorari before judgment is not decided.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Raines's distinction between statutory authorization and actual injury remains; Steel Co.'s priority for disputed Article III questions is observed.

##### Removal of New York's statutory protection creates concrete injury

**Holding:** Cancellation of the identified protection against Medicaid recoupment and consequent provider liability causes a present injury by removing that protection and reviving concrete fiscal exposure, even though waiver proceedings and ultimate payment remain unresolved. A declaration restoring the provision redresses that loss without determining the final debt.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Stevens). All nine Justices join the New York standing part.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Pending waivers and estimated exposure do not establish an assessed debt. One adequate claimant suffices for this action; the unions' separate standing and each additional plaintiff's status are not decided.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Raines is distinguished on actual injury; Lujan's stage-specific factual showing and ordinary redressability requirement are applied.

##### The actual acquisition opportunity supports Snake River's standing

**Holding:** Withdrawal of a seller-side tax incentive creates a cognizable injury to a cooperative's actual, identified acquisition opportunity when supported commercial activity and seller interest permit the inference that the withdrawal impairs that opportunity. Restoration redresses the opportunity loss without guaranteeing a sale, price, financing or eventual tax deferral.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Stevens). Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer join this bounded opportunity holding.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The inference rests on actual acquisition discussions and seller interest, not a promised sale or seller refusal. Consolidation transfers no standing from the New York action. Cranney's independent injury, eventual financing, tax eligibility and completed acquisition remain undecided.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Bryant and Investment Company Institute support opportunity injury at this factual scope; Lujan, Simon and Allen retain actual proof and causation requirements; Akins permits useful relief without guaranteed downstream action.

### Expired preliminary relief and necessary review of an entered dismissal

#### Newton County Wildlife Association v. Rogers

**Docket or dockets:** 97-774; Eighth Circuit Nos. 96-1994 and 96-3463.

**Decided:** 1998-09-28.

##### Completion of the requested plans ends this prospective planning request

**Holding:** A request to stop timber sales only until identified river plans exist is moot once those plans have been completed, when no surviving remedy or concrete recurrence is established for that request. On the equities here, the stale appellate merits treatment must be vacated and the request dismissed; the January 14, 1997 district interpretation remains vacated on mootness grounds.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Ginsburg). Ginsburg's Opinion of the Court, Part I, joined by all nine Justices, supplies direct controlling authority.

**Material application:** The Government reports that the plans were completed and incorporated into adjacent forest plans in October 1996. An order to await that completed work can no longer afford the particular relief requested. The September 30, 1995 deadline was missed, but that historical failure supplies no continuing controversy over whether to stop sales until the plans exist. No surviving damages demand or independent remedy is established for this request.

U.S. Bancorp permits an equitable disposition under §2106 after merits review becomes unavailable; it does not make vacatur automatic. Petitioners neither settled nor abandoned review. The prevailing agency's reported performance exhausted the request before the appellate merits adjudication. Arizonans for Official English permits correction of that stale adjudication without a new merits ruling. These circumstances justify vacatur and dismissal. They also require leaving the January 1997 district interpretation vacated, without adopting the proposition that any interlocutory appeal divests a district court of all related merits authority.

The Court does not excuse the missed deadline, approve the completed plans, decide whether plan preparation was a legal precondition to these sales, or resolve the territorial extent of planning duties. Distinct coordination, adjacency-protection and site-effects obligations are neither extinguished nor newly adjudicated.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** U.S. Bancorp is applied as an equitable, rather than automatic, judgment-clearing rule; Arizonans for Official English is applied to correction of merits treatment after the particular controversy ended.

##### The final adjudication displaces the former preliminary MBTA interval

**Holding:** When final adjudication has ended the period for which preliminary relief was requested, an appeal seeking protection only during that former interval is moot even though an underlying claim may remain reviewable. The obsolete appellate affirmance of preliminary MBTA relief is vacated and that preliminary appeal dismissed; no old preliminary motion is revived.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Ginsburg). Ginsburg's Opinion of the Court, Part II, joined by all nine Justices.

**Material application:** University of Texas v. Camenisch distinguishes temporary preservation of the parties' positions from final adjudication of their rights. The final district-court judgment and April 1, 1998 affirmance ended the pending-trial interval addressed by the July 29, 1996 preliminary order. The Court therefore clears the superseded appellate treatment of that interval rather than deciding whether an injunction should once have issued.

This disposition does not rest on an unsupported finding that every timber operation has finished. Nor does it vacate the later final judgment on other statutory or administrative-record grounds. The actual earlier dismissal of the MBTA claim is a separate component and receives its own review. Any request for future relief on that returned claim must establish a current legal and equitable basis; success in obtaining this limited vacatur supplies none by itself.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Camenisch is applied to the distinct duration and function of preliminary relief. U.S. Bancorp supplies authority for the confined equitable disposition without making vacatur of the independent final adjudications automatic.

##### The entered dismissal was lawfully reviewable with the injunction denial

**Holding:** An injunction appeal permits review of an actually entered and separately appealed dismissal of the same equitable claim when the identical dispositive legal obstacle makes examination of that dismissal necessary to meaningful review and resolving the obstacle requires no additional unresolved factual adjudication. Those conditions are met here; §1254(1) permits review of the MBTA component actually affirmed in the court-of-appeals case without a newly invented appeal or petition.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Ginsburg). Ginsburg's Opinion of the Court, Part III, joined by all nine Justices. Breyer joins this actual Deckert-based formulation; his earlier judgment-only position in Clinton is not treated as a join to Clinton's rationale.

**Material application:** The injunction was refused because the asserted MBTA claim was legally unavailable. One week later the District Court entered a categorical dismissal on that same obstacle, and plaintiffs appealed both orders. Accepting the obstacle would sustain both; rejecting it without correcting the entered dismissal would leave the same claim legally foreclosed. This identity of the legal issue and the equitable claim supplies the necessary connection.

Deckert permits examination of a legal objection defeating the very equitable claim while excluding unrelated interlocutory matters. Swint rejects review of a separate party's independently answerable liability question and reserves genuinely necessary review; this case involves the same entered legal foreclosure. Clinton v. Jones permits review of an integrated temporal-protection order, supplying a confined analogy rather than a general efficiency exception. Camenisch forbids converting a preliminary estimate into final merits adjudication. Here there is an actual dismissal, not an estimate relabeled as a judgment.

Common facts, efficiency and a merits issue's relevance to likelihood of success are insufficient by themselves. Additional independent legal or factual issues remain outside the proper appeal. Later final-judgment merger does not cure an earlier jurisdictional excess, and the Court does not rely on it to do so. Section 1254(1) also does not impose §1257's state-court finality requirement. The later final judgment is posture evidence, not an expanded grant encompassing all its other statutory rulings.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Deckert is applied to the same equitable claim's actual legal dismissal; Swint's separate-liability exclusion is preserved; Clinton's integrated-order principle is applied by bounded analogy; Camenisch's preliminary/final distinction is preserved.

##### The separately dismissed MBTA claim has not been shown moot

**Holding:** Completion of river plans and final adjudication of other statutory claims do not, without loss of all effective relief on the MBTA authorization challenge, moot review of that claim's separate legal dismissal. Review may correct the dismissal on this record, but any affirmative remedy on remand requires a present, independently supported basis and must cease if the claim is actually moot.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Ginsburg). Ginsburg's Opinion of the Court, Part III, joined by all nine Justices.

**Material application:** The controversy concerns identified sale authorizations and alleged injury to protected birds; the Forest Service conceded that some nesting birds would be killed. Final rejection of the claim does not itself extinguish the controversy over that rejection. Neither the reported plan completion nor the later final appeal decided the MBTA claim anew or established that no meaningful relief remains.

The December 1997 report that approximately three-fourths of road work and harvesting was complete proves neither completion by September 1998 nor an exact present amount of unfinished work. The Court infers neither continued cutting nor completed harvesting from the absence of a later report. Arizonans requires a controversy throughout review. Bennett preserves the distinct, stage-appropriate requirements of injury, causation and redressability. Ohio Forestry's premature programmatic challenge differs from these concrete sale authorizations; it supplies no blanket immunity from review and no exemption from the remaining conditions.

The Court decides a legal dismissal and orders no current operational relief. A supported showing on remand that no effective remedy remains must receive its own mootness determination. Conversely, uncertainty about precise harvest status cannot itself establish either mootness or entitlement to an injunction.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Arizonans and Bennett retain the continuing-case and stage-of-proof requirements. Ohio Forestry is distinguished at its absence of a concrete project authorization; its independent jurisdictional and review conditions remain intact.

Related questions in Newton County Wildlife Association v. Rogers, 1998-09-28, dockets 97-774; Eighth Circuit Nos. 96-1994 and 96-3463, appear under Administrative Law — APA review of Forest Service sale authorizations under the MBTA.

### Moot amended election claims and equitable judgment treatment

#### Kentucky Right to Life, Inc. v. Stengel

**Docket or dockets:** 97-60; Sixth Circuit No. 95-6581.

**Decided:** 1998-09-14.

##### The removed applications no longer present a live constitutional dispute

**Holding:** The obsolete §121.180(6)(a) definition-based reporting and §121.035(2) corporate-employee claims, §121.025 corporate independent/issue-spending claim, §121.190(1) issue-only identification claim, and KRL/Zoeller and independent-spending branches of the §121A.030(4) gubernatorial claim are moot because the amendments removed the challenged applications and no surviving injury or supported recurrence remains. The theoretical power to reenact does not preserve those disputes; a credible threat, continuing enforcement or legally available retrospective relief would require separate analysis.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (O'Connor). Part I of O'Connor's Opinion of the Court, joined by all eight other Justices.

**Material application:** The Court holds that the claims must be assessed individually against the law that now operates. The revised permanent-committee definition requires a primary purpose of expressly advocating election or defeat of identified candidates, slates or political parties and regular operation throughout the year; it excludes campaign committees, political issues committees, inaugural committees and party executive committees. Independent expenditures no longer fall within contributions. Section 121.035(3)'s permission for contributions concerning constitutional amendments, ballot public questions and issues of public importance is distinct from its nonprofit independent-expenditure permission, which requires that the nonprofit not derive a substantial portion of its revenue from for-profit corporations. Nothing here decides every nonprofit's eligibility under that condition. The changed text, removed applications and reported enforcement history support mootness, without a surviving damages claim or adverse current application. Arizonans requires a live claim throughout review. The mere possibility of reenactment is insufficient, but an express announcement is not the exclusive way a recurrence concern could be established. No merits conclusion about an obsolete restriction follows from dismissing its challenge.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Arizonans applies the requirement of a continuing controversy and the separation of live relief from dead constitutional questions. City of Mesquite's voluntary-cessation principle remains available on supported recurrence facts; it does not make bare legislative power sufficient or announced reenactment indispensable.

##### Mootness requires only the appropriate nonmerits treatment of the affected judgments

**Holding:** Existing dismissals for want of a controversy remain nonmerits dismissals; underlying merits adjudications of the now-moot applications, including the former issue-advertisement application, are vacated and those claims are to be dismissed as moot. The relief is confined to those applications and does not erase still-live judgments or confer constitutional merits jurisdiction.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (O'Connor). Part II of O'Connor's Opinion of the Court is joined by Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer: eight rationale votes. Stone-Zsela joins only this limited remedial judgment.

**Material application:** The Court holds that jurisdiction and equitable treatment of a judgment are different questions. Bancorp permits a just disposition under §2106 when ordinary merits review becomes unavailable, while denying any automatic entitlement to vacatur. Here the legislature changed the law as the losing challengers pursued review; they did not settle or voluntarily abandon that review. Leaving obsolete constitutional merits determinations in place would retain decisions that cannot now receive the review sought. Arizonans supports clearing such merits rulings while preserving the nonmerits character of jurisdictional dismissals. Those equities justify the limited cleanup, but supply no reason to disturb the independently reviewed live claims. The Court neither approves the repealed scheme nor treats the original merits rulings as void when entered.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Bancorp supplies equitable §2106 authority and retains its settlement-specific limits. Arizonans applies corrective disposition without merits jurisdiction. Munsingwear supports judgment clearing when review becomes unavailable, subject to the equitable inquiry rather than an automatic rule.

Related questions in Kentucky Right to Life, Inc. v. Stengel, 1998-09-14, dockets 97-60; Sixth Circuit No. 95-6581, appear under Election Law — Corporate gifts, paid identification and distinct contribution limits.

### Pre-enforcement campaign-finance review and optional advisory opinions

#### Federal Election Commission v. Maine Right to Life Committee, Inc.

**Docket or dockets:** 96-1818.

**Decided:** 1998-09-14.

##### This live challenge does not require an advisory opinion first

**Holding:** An association whose recurring issue communications are subject to an effective FEC regulation and whose speech has actually ceased because of its credible application may seek pre-enforcement review of the rule's legal reach. Section 437f's permissive advisory-opinion procedure is not a compulsory exhaustion requirement for this challenge and cannot furnish an agency determination invalidating the governing rule.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Stevens). Part I of Stevens's Opinion of the Court is joined by all eight other Justices. All nine join the statutory-design and actual-controversy ground; no broader exhaustion-balancing proposition is adopted.

**Material application:** The Court holds that MRLC need not seek advance official permission for each prospective communication before presenting this facial legal claim. The trial findings establish ongoing speech activity and actual cessation after the regulation became effective, rather than a hypothetical speaker's objection to a dormant rule. McCarthy v. Madigan makes the enacted administrative scheme central; FECA makes this advisory route available without making it mandatory. The remedy sought is a legal decision about the regulation itself, which an advisory opinion cannot invalidate. The association supplies a sufficient controversy for the relief sought, so the Court need not decide Corbett's independent recipient-standing theory. Nor does this conclusion disturb dismissal of the Attorney General, against whom no supported enforcement threat was shown. A materially different administrative scheme or an entirely hypothetical future communication is not decided.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** McCarthy's statutory-primacy principle applies; its broader discretionary balancing is unnecessary here. Its later prison-specific statutory modification does not impose exhaustion under FECA.

Related questions in Federal Election Commission v. Maine Right to Life Committee, Inc., 1998-09-14, dockets 96-1818, appear under Election Law — Contextual express advocacy and statutory limits.

### XII. The general section 208 announcement is not ripe for review

#### AT&T Corp. v. Iowa Utilities Board

**Docket or dockets:** Nos. 97-826, 97-829, 97-830, 97-831, 97-1075, 97-1087, 97-1099 and 97-1141; including California respondents in the AT&T, MCI and FCC petitions.
**Decided:** 1999-01-25.

##### XII. The general section 208 announcement is not ripe for review

**Holding:** A definite agency announcement of a possible enforcement forum is not ripe merely because the position is complete and the dispute predominantly legal. Because this announcement directs no present change in primary conduct, determines no carrier-specific liability and permits meaningful review of a concrete application, the general challenge must be dismissed without prejudice, without deciding enforcement authority.

**Proposition-level authority:** All eight Associate Justices join Scalia's Part XII, supplying a controlling majority. Stone-Zsela agrees only in vacatur of the premature enforcement-merits adjudication and dismissal without prejudice as unripe; he adds no enforcement or ripeness ground.



For the other questions in AT&T Corp. v. Iowa Utilities Board, decided 1999-01-25, at the dockets stated above, see Administrative Law. The complete event-wide precedent treatment and operative mandate appear with this case in Administrative Law.

### I. A live federal claim survives the second referendum

#### Citizens for a Constitutional Convention v. Yoshina

**Docket or dockets:** No. 98-671; Ninth Circuit Nos. 97-16408, 97-16543, 97-16540 and 97-16596.
**Decided:** 1999-01-21.

##### I. A live federal claim survives the second referendum

**Holding:** A later referendum does not moot a substantial federal claim seeking recognition of an asserted entitlement created by the earlier vote when that relief remains capable of addressing a continuing injury. These plaintiffs maintain that claim; their ultimate failure to establish it does not eliminate jurisdiction.

**Proposition-level authority:** Souter's opinion, joined by all eight other Justices, controls this threshold holding and Part II. Part III has the different coalition stated there.



For the other questions in Citizens for a Constitutional Convention v. Yoshina, decided 1999-01-21, at the dockets stated above, see Election Law, First Amendment. The complete event-wide precedent treatment and operative mandate appear with this case in Election Law.

### I. A supported threat to personal representation permits Glavin review

#### Department of Commerce v. United States House of Representatives

**Docket or dockets:** Clinton v. Glavin; Nos. 98-404 and 98-564.
**Decided:** 1999-01-25.

##### I. A supported threat to personal representation permits Glavin review

**Holding:** A voter may challenge an announced census method before the final apportionment when summary-judgment evidence sufficiently supports a concrete threat to that voter's representation, causally attributable to the method and likely redressable by relief against responsible officials. Hofmeister's Indiana representation claim meets that standard; neither statutory authorization alone nor an unsupported prediction would suffice.

**Proposition-level authority:** Souter's Part I, joined by all eight other Justices, controls.

##### II. The House has not established its own institutional injury

**Holding:** Statutory authorization for the House to sue does not establish Article III injury where the asserted harm is disagreement with the lawfulness of a future census methodology, without withheld information or a demonstrated present impairment of institutional power. This House action must be dismissed without prejudice, and its judgment and injunction vacated.

**Proposition-level authority:** Souter's Part II, joined by O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Thomas and Ginsburg, supplies six controlling rationale votes. Stone-Zsela supplies a seventh judgment vote only. Stevens and Breyer dissent from this component.



For the other questions in Department of Commerce v. United States House of Representatives, decided 1999-01-25, at the dockets stated above, see Constitutional Structure. The complete event-wide precedent treatment and operative mandate appear with this case in Constitutional Structure.

### Standing for this official-use controversy

#### Fraternal Order of Police v. United States

**Docket or dockets:** D.C. Circuit No. 97-5304; judgment below, 152 F.3d 998.
**Decided:** 1998-10-07.

##### Standing for this official-use controversy

**Holding:** An association may seek prospective, uniform relief against this official-use exclusion when its chief law-enforcement members suffer a concrete inability lawfully to arm affected officers, the claim is germane to the association, and the requested relief requires no individualized member participation. The chiefs' direct exposure under the supply prohibition and their relationship to the affected officers also permit assertion of the officers' equality interest in this controversy; no injury supporting every individual private-possession or restoration claim is established.

**Proposition-level authority:** Souter's unanimous opinion of the Court.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The holding concerns the developed official-use controversy, not every privately held weapon of a public employee. No individual conviction-classification or restoration ruling, private-possession application, or unresolved commerce or commandeering ground is decided. Section 928 does not authorize extending an exemption the developed objections do not invalidate.

For the other questions in Fraternal Order of Police v. United States, decided 1998-10-07, D.C. Circuit No. 97-5304; judgment below, 152 F.3d 998., see Civil Rights.

### I. The continuing personal controversy requires resolution

#### Gable v. Patton / Patton v. Gable

**Docket or dockets:** Nos. 98-897 and 98-1072.
**Decided:** 1999-03-04.

##### I. The continuing personal controversy requires resolution

**Holding:** When a postjudgment dispute leaves unresolved whether an election-law challenger retains a concrete personal stake in the challenged provisions, the lower courts must determine continuing exposure and redressability before deciding the constitutional merits. Supported present deterrence from candidacy or a reasonable expectation of renewed candidacy may establish that stake without formal filing; an unchanged statute and short election cycles alone cannot supply the same plaintiff's missing personal interest.

**Proposition-level authority:** Souter's Opinion of the Court is joined in full by O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas. Those five Justices directly support this rule and its application; the four dissenters would find the personal showing sufficient now.

##### II. Vacatur and remand are confined to the granted components

**Holding:** The appellate judgment is vacated only as to approval of the responsive-financing trigger in No. 98-897 and invalidation of the twenty-eight-day prohibition on new personal funds in No. 98-1072, and those components are remanded for the continuing-controversy determination. If the controversy remains live, the lower courts may proceed under effective law; if it does not, they must decide the equitable treatment of the respective lower judgments in light of the actual cause and timing of mootness and the cross-petition's requested correction.

**Proposition-level authority:** The same five-Justice Court opinion supplies the complete remedial rationale and both docket-specific dispositions.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- **Lujan v. Defenders of Wildlife, June 12, 1992:** Applied to concrete personal injury, causation, redressability and stage-appropriate support. Its requirements remain intact; no formal-candidacy prerequisite is added.
- **Department of Commerce v. House of Representatives / Clinton v. Glavin, January 25, 1999:** Applied to the actual claimant's supported personal exposure and the distinction between a concrete threatened injury and an abstract objection. One party's desire for a ruling cannot supply another's missing personal stake.
- **Day v. Holahan, May 15, 1995:** Its fractured responsive-financing judgment is not converted into a controlling financing rationale. Its separate notice, intermediary-contribution and nonprofit holdings remain unaffected.
- **Buckley v. Valeo, 424 U.S. 1; Colorado Republican Federal Campaign Committee v. Federal Election Commission, June 26, 1996; Kentucky Right to Life v. Stengel, September 14, 1998; City of Cincinnati v. Kruse, December 16, 1998; Suster v. Marshall, January 28, 1999; and Buckley v. American Constitutional Law Foundation, January 12, 1999:** Their contribution, expenditure, public-financing and election-law rules are neither extended nor displaced. The Court reaches no financing or personal-funds merits; the dissent's application of those authorities is noncontrolling.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Personal injury, causation and redressability remain required throughout review. Supported present deterrence or reasonably expected renewed candidacy can establish a continuing personal stake without a formal filing, but the present dispute requires lower-court resolution. The appellate judgment is vacated only within the two granted components. The Court establishes no new rule on responsive financing, own-funded expression, outside contributions or severability. The existing financing and expenditure precedents retain their separate scopes, and the dissent's merits views do not govern the remand.

**Operative remedy or transition:** **Vacated within the granted components and remanded, 5–4 on each docket.**

**Supporting both dispositions:** O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter and Thomas.
**Opposing both dispositions:** Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Ginsburg and Breyer.

In No. 98-897, the vacatur concerns approval of the responsive-financing trigger. In No. 98-1072, it concerns invalidation of the twenty-eight-day prohibition on newly supplied personal funds. Neither constitutional judgment is affirmed or reversed on its merits. The lower courts must determine Gable's continuing personal exposure to each challenged provision and redressability, using supported existing material or ordinary properly received jurisdictional submissions. If the controversy remains live, they may proceed under effective law. If it does not, they must decide the equitable treatment of the respective lower judgments in light of the actual cause and timing of mootness and the cross-petition's requested correction. This order neither finds mootness nor automatically vacates every district-court ruling. The ungranted components receive no new disposition.

### The preliminary-period authority controversy remains live

#### Grupo Mexicano de Desarrollo, S.A. v. Alliance Bond Fund, Inc.

**Docket or dockets:** No. 98-231.
**Decided:** 1999-06-17.

##### The preliminary-period authority controversy remains live

**Holding:** When a preliminary restraint is challenged as beyond the court's legal power during the period before judgment, later success on the underlying contract and conversion to a permanent injunction do not necessarily extinguish that distinct controversy. Here the asserted preliminary-period injury and existing bond preserve a consequential authority question, while actual bond liability and damages remain undecided.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's Part I is joined by all nine Justices and controls at this limited level of generality.

##### General equity does not supply this unsecured-creditor asset freeze

**Holding:** A federal court may not use general equitable authority, at the request of a general unsecured creditor suing solely for money damages, to freeze a defendant's unencumbered assets before judgment when the creditor has no lien, equitable interest, statutory authorization or other recognized source of prejudgment control. Likely success and threatened inability to collect do not supply the missing power.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's Part II is joined by Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Kennedy and Thomas; five Justices adopt the complete rule, explanation and remedy.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The decision does not restrict injunctions protecting specific property in dispute, statutory freezes, independently authorized fraudulent-transfer remedies, bankruptcy orders, Rule 64 attachment or postjudgment enforcement. It does not decide bond recovery, create a lien, establish fraud or adjudicate the rights of absent Mexican creditors.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- University of Texas v. Camenisch: applies the distinction between a surviving case and the precise preliminary issue; the distinct legal-power consequence here survives without a present bond-liability adjudication.
- De Beers Consolidated Mines v. United States: applies its limit connecting preliminary relief to recognized authority and the permissible final remedy; it supports rejection of this unsecured-creditor freeze.
- Deckert v. Independence Shares Corp.: distinguishes its equitable rescission setting; its preservation authority remains intact within that setting.
- United States v. First National City Bank: distinguishes its federal-tax and statutory predicates; personal jurisdiction and preservation language do not alone establish private unsecured-creditor power.
- Mertens v. Hewitt Associates: preserves its statutory equitable-category rule; it does not itself govern every private injunction or prohibit all equitable monetary remedies.
- Varity and Peacock: preserve their ERISA rules concerning appropriate individual equity and an independently violated obligation; neither supplies this private-creditor power.
- Department of the Army v. Blue Fox: preserves the distinction between an equitable label and an actual legal entitlement within its sovereign/APA setting.
- West v. Gibson: leaves intact the EEOC's specifically enacted compensation authority; it creates no general private-creditor injunction power.

**Limits and questions not reached:** General federal equity supplies no prejudgment security freeze on the stated unsecured, purely monetary claim without an independent recognized source of control. Equity's flexibility, legally authorized attachment and statutory or proprietary remedies retain their respective scopes. Postjudgment enforcement remains distinct. A surviving preliminary-period authority issue may remain reviewable despite later final relief, but neither a bond nor success on the contract automatically determines bond liability.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The live-controversy ruling is unanimous. Reversal and remand are 5–4. Supporting: Stone-Zsela, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas. Opposing: Stevens, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer, who would affirm the reviewed preliminary judgment.

The preliminary order ceased upon conversion on April 17, 1998; it may not continue or be reinstated on the rejected general-creditor theory. The Court does not dissolve the distinct permanent injunction, disturb the final money judgment or decide the separate turnover appeal. Any actual bond claim remains subject to proof of wrongful restraint, causation and loss and to available defenses; no damages are awarded. Attachment, fraudulent-transfer, receivership, bankruptcy and other remedies remain available only through their independently established legal sources and predicates.

### I. Qualification ends this federal controversy

#### Hart v. Gwadosky

**Docket or dockets:** No. 98-676; Maine Supreme Judicial Court No. Ken-98-280.
**Decided:** 1999-02-11.

##### I. Qualification ends this federal controversy

**Holding:** A challenge seeking relief from an initiative's failure to qualify becomes moot when the initiative qualifies and the claimant shows neither a surviving redressable injury nor a reasonable expectation of renewed exposure to the challenged restriction. The exception for a dispute capable of repetition yet evading review requires both duration too short for complete review and reasonably expected renewed exposure of the same claimant; the continuing existence of an election rule alone does not satisfy it.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part I of O'Connor's Opinion of the Court is joined by all eight other Justices. These nine directly support the claimant-specific mootness holding and its application.

##### II. Equitable vacatur leaves state proceedings to Maine

**Holding:** When a changed qualification threshold removes this controversy while petitioners continue seeking qualification and review, section 2106 permits equitable vacatur of the reviewed constitutional judgment and remand for proceedings allowed by state law. Vacatur is justified here by those circumstances, rather than by an automatic entitlement whenever review becomes moot; Article III does not require an order directing dismissal of the state action.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part II of O'Connor's Opinion of the Court is joined by Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer; those eight Associates directly support its equitable rationale. Stone-Zsela agrees in the vacatur and bounded state remand without joining Part II's additional equitable explanation.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- **Spencer v. Kemna, March 3, 1998:** Applied to continuing personal injury and both requirements of the recurrence exception; neither is relaxed for this election dispute.
- **Norman v. Reed, January 14, 1992:** Distinguished because its continuing party-qualification consequences are absent here; its supported-election-continuity rule remains intact.
- **Chandler v. Miller, April 15, 1997:** Distinguished because its uncontested future candidacy supplied renewed exposure; no comparable further initiative plan is established here.
- **Libertarian Party of Illinois v. Rednour, September 21, 1998:** Distinguished at its continuing nomination-classification scope; completion of one election and disappearance of this initiative's qualification injury present different circumstances.
- **Arizonans for Official English v. Arizona, March 3, 1997:** Applied to the need for an actual surviving claim and the unavailability of a manufactured official-capacity damages residue; its direction to dismiss a federal action is not extended to a state action.
- **U.S. Bancorp Mortgage Co. v. Bonner Mall Partnership, November 8, 1994:** Applied to equitable section 2106 relief; no automatic-vacatur rule or new settlement exception is adopted.
- **Kentucky Right to Life v. Stengel, September 14, 1998:** Applied to separate jurisdictional mootness from equitable judgment treatment after changed circumstances; its independent live-claim rulings are unaffected.
- **DeFunis v. Odegaard, 416 U.S. 312:** Applied to federal mootness on review of a state judgment and remand for appropriate state proceedings; state-law authority is not displaced by Article III.
- **Buckley v. American Constitutional Law Foundation, January 12, 1999:** Its express reservation of residence requirements is preserved. Its Colorado registration holding is neither extended to Maine residency nor reconsidered.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The continuing-injury and equitable-vacatur requirements remain in force. This qualified initiative supplies no live federal controversy on the presented record, and the reviewed constitutional judgment is vacated. Maine's authority over further state proceedings is preserved. Residence and registration restrictions, signature validity and the initiative's later legislative or electoral fate remain undecided. Buckley's residence reservation is unchanged.

**Operative remedy or transition:** **Vacated and remanded, 9–0.** The federal controversy is moot. The Maine Supreme Judicial Court's reviewed judgment is vacated, and the cause is remanded for such proceedings as state law provides.

**Supporting:** Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer.

The mandate does not direct dismissal of the state action under Article III. Residence, registration and signature validity are not decided. Administrative qualification remains undisturbed; no new counting or ballot-certification order issues.

### The continuing employment injury supports standing and present review

#### Hiley v. Barrett

**Docket or dockets:** Eleventh Circuit No. 97-9006; 155 F.3d 1276. No separately established Supreme Court docket.
**Decided:** 1998-10-09.

##### The continuing employment injury supports standing and present review

**Holding:** Hiley's continuing exclusion from armed-deputy work, traceable to application of the federal disability and redressable through the settlement's restoration-if-successful condition, establishes a concrete controversy despite his reassignment to detention work. NAGE may pursue this uniform prospective legal challenge through affected members where the claim is germane to its representative purpose and neither the claim nor the requested relief requires individual participation; the already operative injury makes the controversy ripe.

**Proposition-level authority:** O'Connor's opinion of the Court, joined by Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer; eight direct rationale votes. Stone-Zsela agrees in the judgments without joining the opinion.

##### Personal arms protection does not revive abandoned claims

**Holding:** The recorded abandonment of strict-scrutiny review and the separately recorded abandonment of procedural due process limit this review; rejecting the lower court's collective-only arms premise neither withdraws those abandonments nor creates a separately preserved Second Amendment claim. The surviving federal challenges remain for decision, while claims against the county and sheriff settled and dismissed with prejudice remain closed.

**Proposition-level authority:** O'Connor's opinion of the Court, joined by Stevens, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer; eight direct rationale votes. Stone-Zsela agrees in the judgments without joining the opinion.

**Material application:** *Love*, *Hale* and *Rybar* recognize personal invocation of the arms guarantee independently of militia affiliation. *Wright* supplies Core-Preservation Review: first determine constitutional coverage, then measure justification against the severity of the restriction's practical burden. *Fraternal Order of Police* applies that rule to a qualifying domestic-violence disability as a substantial regulation satisfying intermediate scrutiny, and holds that personal protection does not require an official-use exception. A preserved Second Amendment challenge here would be governed by that framework and application.

Hiley abandoned the arms claim, and the incorporated district order's footnotes 11 and 16 separately record the abandonments of strict-scrutiny review and procedural due process. The Court rejects the collective-only premise without withdrawing those abandonments. It decides the surviving equality and employment theories at their preserved scope; it does not apply intermediate scrutiny to an unpreserved arms claim, decide Hiley's private-possession rights, or reopen the local settlement. The reference to *Wright* and *Fraternal Order of Police* identifies governing law rather than an additional holding on the abandoned claim.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The case decides the preserved civil challenges and no criminal prosecution, notice or mental-state defense. No separate abandoned strict-scrutiny or procedural-due-process claim is revived; the local claims settled with prejudice stay closed. No individual conviction-relief entitlement, never-lost-rights construction or universal firearms disability is established.

For the other questions in Hiley v. Barrett, decided 1998-10-09, Eleventh Circuit No. 97-9006; 155 F.3d 1276. No separately established Supreme Court docket., see Constitutional Structure, Civil Rights.

### 1. The Natural Law Party committee's policy challenge is capable of repetition yet evading review

#### Marcus v. Iowa Public Television

**Docket or dockets:** No. 98-710.
**Decided:** 1999-01-13.

##### 1. The Natural Law Party committee's policy challenge is capable of repetition yet evading review

**Holding:** A challenge to completed pre-election broadcasts remains justiciable for the same continuing party committee when its established relationship to several excluded candidates, general electoral function, the broadcaster's general selection practice and its recurring program support a reasonable expectation of another short candidate-access controversy involving that committee. Both the period too short for complete review and the same claimant's reasonable prospect of recurrence are required; neither election timing nor an abstract organizational purpose alone suffices.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part I of O'Connor's opinion is joined by Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer: eight Justices. Stone-Zsela agrees that the Court reaches the merits but does not join this threshold explanation.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The surviving common claim does not separately preserve every individual's expired demand, establish future standing for every named plaintiff or supply an independent current injury from program-guide language. There is no general election exception, relaxed jurisdiction because review was granted, presumed reputational consequence or order to recreate a past broadcast.



For the other questions in Marcus v. Iowa Public Television, decided 1999-01-13, at the dockets stated above, see First Amendment. The complete event-wide precedent treatment and operative mandate appear with this case in First Amendment.

### The lender's pecuniary stake permits appellate review

#### McClellan Federal Credit Union v. Parker

**Docket or dockets:** No. 98-618.
**Decided:** 1998-12-09.

##### The lender's pecuniary stake permits appellate review

**Holding:** The credit union is sufficiently pecuniarily aggrieved to appeal refusal of this proposed reaffirmation because approval would make the proposed personal car-loan and $1,500 unsecured obligations enforceable. That appellate stake permits review but does not establish a right to approval or remove §524's conditions.

**Proposition-level authority:** Thomas's unanimous opinion of the Court.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The valid lien and discharged personal liability retain separate consequences. No default, foreclosure, repossession or substitute agreement is ordered, and no general invalidity of bankruptcy clauses is decided. Section 521(2) does not itself excuse an actual default or authorize lien stripping or installment redemption.

For the other questions in McClellan Federal Credit Union v. Parker, decided 1998-12-09, No. 98-618., see Bankruptcy.

### Actual Article III standing before contested constitutional merits

#### NAACP, Los Angeles Branch v. Jones

**Docket or dockets:** No. 97-1840; Ninth Circuit No. 96-56455; judgment below, 131 F.3d 1317.
**Decided:** 1998-11-05.

##### Actual Article III standing before contested constitutional merits

**Holding:** A federal court may not assume disputed Article III standing to decide these contested constitutional claims about judicial-election financing and optional candidate statements. It must determine, with support appropriate to the litigation stage, whether the plaintiffs have a concrete and particularized, actual or imminent injury fairly traceable to the challenged conduct and likely to be redressed by the prospective relief they seek; the Ninth Circuit's merits judgment is vacated so that determination can be made on the actual pleadings and appropriate record.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's unanimous opinion of the Court.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The lower courts must determine standing on the actual pleadings and appropriate record before any merits ruling. The Court orders no particular evidentiary hearing, standing discovery, document production or amendment entitlement. It neither approves the optional charge nor requires campaign financing or free publication. No indigent-candidate claim is decided, and the unpreserved state-law claims are not revived. No finding of racial intent or viewpoint discrimination, new future-candidacy allegation, ballot-access ruling or rule for a compulsory filing fee is supplied. Merits arguments about the wealth-primary theory and optional printing costs are not alternative holdings.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Neither standing nor its absence is found. No hearing, discovery, production or amendment entitlement is ordered. Both election-financing and optional-statement constitutional merits remain unadjudicated; no indigent-candidate, compulsory filing-fee, racial-intent or viewpoint-discrimination holding follows, and unpreserved state-law claims are not revived.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- *Citizens for a Better Environment v. Steel Co.*, June 2, 1997: applied to prohibit assumed disputed Article III standing as a basis for these contested constitutional merits; its nonexhaustive treatment of threshold sequencing remains intact. Its direction to dismiss on the different completed-injury record is not transferred here.
- *Lujan v. Defenders of Wildlife*, 504 U.S. 555 (1992): applied for personal injury, traceability, redressability and support appropriate to the litigation stage; its summary-judgment evidentiary demand is not imposed on these pleadings, and its noncontrolling absent-agency application is not enlarged.
- *Bennett v. Spear*, March 19, 1997: applied for stage-appropriate pleading of causal and remedial links; its supported ongoing injury does not establish injury in this different complaint.
- *Norton v. Mathews*, 427 U.S. 524 (1976): preserved at its already-controlled-merits setting; it supplies no permission to assume standing for this unresolved constitutional dispute.
- *Secretary of the Navy v. Avrech*, 418 U.S. 676 (1974): likewise preserved at its distinct already-controlled-merits setting; the Court does not extend that exception to perceived merits simplicity.
- *Libertarian Party of Florida v. Smith*, September 21, 1998: distinguished because its actual-burden review of a different subsidy resolves neither this standing question nor the optional-statement merits. Its election-law limits remain unchanged.

### III. Invalidating this affiliation maximum would not redress the asserted representation injury

#### National Committee of the Reform Party of the United States of America v. Democratic National Committee

**Docket or dockets:** Ninth Circuit No. 98-15443; 168 F.3d 360.
**Decided:** 1999-03-17.

##### III. Invalidating this affiliation maximum would not redress the asserted representation injury

**Holding:** These plaintiffs lack standing to challenge the FEC's maximum of three members affiliated with one party because they identify no concrete exclusion caused by that maximum that its removal would likely redress. The jurisdictional dismissal decides neither the cap's constitutional merits nor a different discriminatory appointment or enforcement claim.

**Proposition-level authority:** Thomas's Part III, joined by all eight other Justices, controls the affiliation-cap standing holding.

##### IV. The granted claims supply no basis for bypassing or requiring a different statutory tribunal

**Holding:** A claim lacking Article III standing or presenting a constitutional theory plainly foreclosed by controlling law may be rejected before invoking the extraordinary statutory tribunal. A live substantial constitutional question cannot be bypassed merely because a court expects it ultimately to fail; a substantial FECA question follows section 437h, while a substantial Fund Act implementation or construction claim follows section 9011(b).

**Proposition-level authority:** Thomas, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer join Part IV. Stone-Zsela does not join it.



For the other questions in National Committee of the Reform Party of the United States of America v. Democratic National Committee, decided 1999-03-17, at the dockets stated above, see Election Law. The complete event-wide precedent treatment and operative mandate appear with this case in Election Law.

### The identified owners may seek present review

#### Peoples Rights Organization, Inc. v. City of Columbus

**Docket or dockets:** Sixth Circuit Nos. 96-3468 and 96-3495; 152 F.3d 522. No separately established Supreme Court docket.
**Decided:** 1998-10-15.

##### The identified owners may seek present review

**Holding:** Owners who identify existing possession affected by a criminal ordinance, a closed exemption window and an express enforcement threat may obtain pre-enforcement review without inviting prosecution. P.R.O. may seek the common prospective relief through affected members whose injury is concrete, whose interests are germane to its purpose, and whose claims and requested relief require no individualized participation.

**Proposition-level authority:** Thomas's opinion of the Court; O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer join in full, and Stevens joins every part except Part IX. Eight direct joins support this proposition unless Part IX is specified.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No substantive Second Amendment judgment, prosecution, automatic arrest authorization or new registration opportunity follows. No enforcement may rest independently on either invalid modification branch; valid definitions remain subject to the distinct enacted fault condition. Actual Ohio severability determines the conforming declaratory and injunctive relief.

For the other questions in Peoples Rights Organization, Inc. v. City of Columbus, decided 1998-10-15, Sixth Circuit Nos. 96-3468 and 96-3495; 152 F.3d 522. No separately established Supreme Court docket., see Civil Rights.

### Credible personal injury and effective relief remain necessary

#### Wisconsin Right to Life, Inc. v. Paradise

**Docket or dockets:** No. 98-118; Seventh Circuit No. 97-3668.
**Decided:** 1998-11-24.

##### Credible personal injury and effective relief remain necessary

**Holding:** A speaker seeking pre-enforcement relief must establish concrete actual or imminent personal injury, fairly traceable to a defendant and likely to be redressed by the requested relief, with support appropriate to the litigation stage; a completed prosecution is unnecessary when an objectively credible threat burdens the speaker's intended expression. Self-censorship, a facial label or the existence of private enforcement provisions alone does not establish those requirements, and a threat from independent private parties must be connected to relief lawfully available against the defendants before the court.

**Proposition-level authority:** Chief Justice Stone-Zsela's opinion of the Court, joined by Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer (seven Justices).

##### The actual facial claim must receive the correct jurisdictional inquiry

**Holding:** A court evaluating this facial invalidity and nonenforcement request may not dispose of the entire action solely by treating the requested relief as a command prescribing future state-court construction, or by treating predicted failure of an otherwise substantial constitutional claim as lack of jurisdiction. The Seventh Circuit must reconsider the actual injuries, defendants and available relief; contested merits and the District Court's abstention ruling remain for proper consideration only after the necessary Article III inquiry.

**Proposition-level authority:** Chief Justice Stone-Zsela's opinion of the Court, joined by Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer (seven Justices).

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Court neither finds standing nor affirms the District Court's standing determination; it does not decide Pullman abstention or invalidate Wisconsin law. Official and independent private enforcement have distinct causal and remedial requirements. Contested merits remain for proper consideration only after the necessary Article III inquiry.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Lujan v. Defenders of Wildlife: applies the controlling personal-interest, traceability, redressability and stage-specific support rules; its noncontrolling absent-agency application is not adopted.
- Babbitt v. United Farm Workers: preserves pre-enforcement review of a credible personal threat without a completed prosecution.
- Virginia v. American Booksellers Ass'n: preserves supported pre-enforcement speech injury; the speech setting does not eliminate Article III requirements.
- Federal Election Commission v. Maine Right to Life Committee, Inc. (September 14, 1998): applies the distinction between a credible effective speech constraint and an unthreatening defendant. Its contextual express-advocacy holding remains governing and is not replaced by an exclusive verbal formula; its unresolved constitutional questions remain open.
- Bennett v. Spear: applies the need for a supported defendant-linked causal and remedial connection without demanding certainty about every later choice.
- Pic-A-State Pennsylvania, Inc. v. Reno: preserves meaningful partial relief from an actual constraint; not every independent barrier must disappear.
- Lee v. Harcleroad: applies the actual-duty, present-burden and proper-defendant inquiry without finding jurisdiction or ordering an automatic hearing.
- Citizens for a Better Environment v. Steel Co.: applies the distinction between Article III power and disputed merits; no exhaustive order for all nonmerits issues is prescribed.
- NAACP, Los Angeles Branch v. Jones (November 5, 1998): applies its requirement of actual standing adjudication before contested election-law merits.
- Baker v. General Motors Corp.: preserves lawful judgment reach and the requirements for binding absent persons; facial relief is not universal preclusion.
- Railroad Commission v. Pullman Co.: leaves its distinct abstention inquiry available for proper consideration after jurisdiction is addressed; its application here is not decided.

### Environmental standing and voluntary cessation

#### Friends of the Earth, Inc. v. Laidlaw Environmental Services (TOC), Inc., 528 U.S. 167 (2000)

**Docket or dockets:** No. 98-822.
**Decided:** January 12, 2000.

##### Whether supported curtailment of local river use establishes personal injury without separate environmental damage

**Holding:** Supported curtailment of identified local recreational and aesthetic use because of reasonable concern about ongoing mercury discharges is a concrete personal injury; Article III does not require separate proof of environmental damage when the asserted injury is this loss of use and enjoyment. General interest in compliance or unexplained subjective concern alone remains insufficient.

**Proposition-level authority:** O'Connor's opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer; seven Justices.

**Material application:** The supported current local-use interests and curtailment differ from indefinite distant-travel intentions. Childhood cessation for another reason, limited prior visits and scientific no-harm findings retain their actual weight; none is converted into proof of frequent historical use or automatically negates reasonable present-use loss.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No permit violation injures everyone downstream, no property valuation is decided, and no separate Sierra Club standing determination is needed for the identical request supported by other organizations' injured members.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Lujan requires concrete personal interests and stage-appropriate support. Brown Group separates representative enforcement authority from members' personal stakes.


##### Whether Treasury civil penalties can redress threatened personal injury through deterrence

**Holding:** A statutorily authorized civil penalty payable to the Treasury can provide Article III redress when it is likely to deter continuing or reasonably recurring violations affecting the plaintiff's supported personal interests. Payment to the plaintiff is unnecessary for that preventive effect, but punishment of wholly past conduct without a remaining remedial connection, satisfaction from punishment and fees alone are insufficient.

**Proposition-level authority:** O'Connor's opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer; seven Justices.

**Material application:** Violations were ongoing at filing, with subsequent mercury exceedances. The statutory penalty can deter recurrence affecting these use interests despite existing governmental enforcement and other litigation exposure; certainty is unnecessary.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Steel Co. is distinguished from its post-cure suit with no continuing or imminent violation alleged. Its express deterrence reservation applies, while its completed-injury limitation remains.


##### Whether compliance and later facility closure establish mootness

**Holding:** A defendant relying on voluntary cessation must make it absolutely clear that the challenged injurious conduct cannot reasonably be expected to recur. Substantial compliance sufficient to defeat an injunction does not automatically satisfy that burden or erase standing that existed at filing.

**Proposition-level authority:** O'Connor's opinion, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer; seven Justices.

**Material application:** The lower court must assess actual closure, dismantling, sale, retained permit and consent-agreement limits. The agreement stopped incineration while allowing approved equivalent treatment capacity subject to permits and reconstruction conditions. Neither permit retention nor dismantling alone decides recurrence; alleged reporting problems do not establish resumed mercury discharge.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Court does not newly affirm or increase the $405,800 penalty, adjudicate the preserved diligent-prosecution defense, award fees, reopen the unappealed denial of an injunction, or decide the unpresented Article II question. The dispute over whether the liability ruling itself was declaratory relief does not establish a separate declaratory decree in the record. Fees neither cure missing standing nor alone prevent mootness. Broader survival of a properly initiated penalty claim after proven permanent cessation is reserved by the Court.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Reverse the appellate vacatur and dismissal direction. The district penalty judgment returns to ordinary unresolved appellate review, including diligent prosecution, closure and penalty questions, without affirmance of its merits or amount. Ordinary mandate and actual stay rules govern collection; no immediate execution, new injunction, fee award or special stay follows.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** The established voluntary-cessation burden remains distinct from entitlement to prospective equitable relief.

### Mootness and continuing competitive injury

#### Adarand Constructors, Inc. v. Slater, 528 U.S. 216 (2000)

**Docket or dockets:** No. 99-295.
**Decided:** January 12, 2000.

##### Whether unsettled federal acceptance of state certification moots an established bidding controversy

**Holding:** When an able and ready bidder with concrete continuing exposure challenges unequal competitive treatment, the party relying on postfiling cessation must adequately establish that the challenged disadvantage will not reasonably recur. Adarand's state certification does not carry that showing because its federal acceptance and durable effect remain unsettled; this conclusion does not relax initial standing or stage-appropriate proof, and a supported durable elimination of prospective disadvantage could require a different mootness determination.

**Proposition-level authority:** Per curiam opinion, unanimous.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The certificate's unresolved federal acceptance and continuing effect, together with concrete repeated bidding, defeat mootness; reviewability alone does not create perpetual jurisdiction. No withdrawal, present ineligibility, program invalidity, or entitlement to a contract is found. Peña's common substantial-justification standard and bounded mandate remain unchanged.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Reverse the appellate mootness judgment and remand for preserved issues and the district decree's lawful effect under the June 12, 1995 mandate. The reversal does not affirm the district court's constitutional reasoning or automatically restore all relief; no new complaint, injunction, damages or replacement program is ordered.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Adarand Constructors, Inc. v. Peña (June 12, 1995): applies its equal-opportunity standing holding and preserves its common substantial-justification standard and bounded vacatur/remand; no program-merits question is resolved here.
- Northeastern Florida Chapter, Associated General Contractors v. Jacksonville (June 14, 1993): applies its distinction between changes continuing the challenged exclusion and changes actually ending it; no guaranteed contract or automatic injunction follows.
- City of Mesquite v. Aladdin's Castle, Inc., 455 U.S. 283 (1982): applies the surviving voluntary-cessation principle; the challenged injury must actually be removed with adequate assurance against recurrence.
- Lujan v. Defenders of Wildlife (June 12, 1992): retains concrete injury, sufficiently imminent renewed exposure and stage-appropriate proof; cessation analysis does not replace initial standing.
- Texas v. Lesage (November 29, 1999): applies the distinction between unequal-process injury and entitlement to particular relief; continued jurisdiction does not establish a remedial entitlement.

### Continuing adversity in review of a state judgment

#### City of Erie v. Pap's A.M., 529 U.S. 277 (2000)

**Docket or dockets:** No. 98-1161.

**Decided:** March 29, 2000.

For the public-nudity holding in City of Erie v. Pap's A.M., March 29, 2000, No. 98-1161, see First Amendment — Communicative targeting in public-nudity regulation.

##### Whether the continuing state judgment and adverse defense preserve this federal controversy after the business closes

**Holding:** In this state-review posture, the judgment's continuing withdrawal of the City's enforcement authority, coupled with the respondent's continuing adverse defense of that judgment, supplies a live federal controversy despite Pap's cessation of the regulated business. This rule depends on the actual legal disability and adversity; abstract governmental interest, incorporation, fees or an imagined return to business does not suffice.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stevens's Part I, joined by O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer; six direct rationale votes. Stone-Zsela separately votes to reach the merits without adopting Part I; Scalia and Thomas dissent from reaching the merits.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Closure, sale and retirement are accepted. Incorporation, fees, speculative reopening and nonexistent monetary claims do not supply the required controversy. This is no general public-law exception to a continuing personal controversy, and the defendant-cessation burden is not imposed on a plaintiff leaving its business.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Reach the federal merits, 7–2. The threshold dissent would dismiss federal review without automatic erasure of the state judgment; the Court does not assert a federal power to vacate a state decree merely because federal review ends.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** ASARCO supplies injury from a state judgment for the party first invoking federal review, while retaining the case-or-controversy requirement. Arizonans for Official English retains its actual-expiration rule. Laidlaw retains its separate injury, redressability and defendant-cessation predicates; Bancorp supplies no automatic entitlement to erase a state judgment.

### Pre-enforcement review of electoral speech restrictions

#### North Carolina Right to Life, Inc. v. Bartlett

**Docket or dockets:** No separate Supreme Court docket supplied; review of 168 F.3d 705 (4th Cir. 1999).
**Decided:** May 1, 2000.

##### The actual threat and cessation of speech permit this pre-enforcement challenge

**Holding:** NCRL may obtain review of the challenged coverage before prosecution where its intended continuing speech encounters penal statutory language, an official application to its proposed guide and actual cessation, without a binding construction removing the threat. This record supplies no basis to require referral of the statutory question to the Elections Board or to abstain from the two granted questions.

**Proposition-level authority:** Souter's Opinion of the Court, joined throughout by all eight other Justices. All nine adopt the three holdings at their stated scopes.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The continuing-speech dispute rests on an official application, penal exposure and actual cessation. The Court imposes no universal anti-abstention rule. The record supplied neither a binding narrowing commitment nor an available certification route that required a different disposition.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Maine Right to Life supplies review of a recurring speech controversy without mandatory use of an optional advisory procedure. Akins concerns a distinct informational-standing injury and does not displace this speaker’s own-injury basis.

For the political-committee and expenditure coverage holdings in North Carolina Right to Life v. Bartlett, May 1, 2000, review of 168 F.3d 705 (no Supreme Court docket supplied), see Election Law — Political-committee and independent-issue-expenditure coverage.

### Mootness and equitable vacatur after voluntary abandonment

#### City News & Novelty, Inc. v. City of Waukesha, 531 U.S. 278

**Docket or dockets:** No. 99-1680
**Decided:** January 17, 2001.

##### Whether abandonment of the business and license request leaves a live prospective controversy

**Holding:** A former licensee who withdraws renewal, abandons the regulated business and establishes no concrete plan to return or surviving claim has no continuing personal stake in prospective review of the licensing procedure. Refusal to promise permanent nonreturn does not restore that stake; this writ must therefore be dismissed as moot without deciding the granted First Amendment question.

**Proposition-level authority:** Unanimous per curiam opinion.

**Material application:** The renewal request and adult business were abandoned with no concrete plan to return or surviving claim. The existing licensing disabilities expired in 2000; the asserted later disability depends on a violation finding never made. No reasonably likely recurrence of short-lived action for this applicant is established.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The defendant-cessation burden does not govern the claimant's own abandonment. The First Amendment does not excuse the continuing-stake requirement. Prompt judicial access versus determination and interim operation during review remain undecided.

##### Whether voluntary loss of review warrants vacatur of the state judgment

**Holding:** Dismissal for mootness does not automatically require vacatur of the judgment under review; the party seeking that equitable relief must justify it in the actual circumstances. City News voluntarily abandoned the activity and application that made review useful and establishes no further sufficient equity, so the Wisconsin judgment is left intact without affirmance of its federal merits reasoning.

**Proposition-level authority:** Unanimous per curiam opinion.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Dismiss the writ as moot and leave the Wisconsin judgment in place, without affirming its federal reasoning. The applicant's voluntary abandonment and absence of further sufficient equities do not justify vacatur. No licensing declaration or injunction issues.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Article III limits this Court's review without retroactively determining the Wisconsin court's jurisdiction; no exhaustive list of vacatur equities or new proof threshold is adopted.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Arizonans for Official English v. Arizona: applies its continuing-personal-stake rule; distinguishes the equities supporting corrective vacatur of lower federal judgments in that case.
- Friends of the Earth v. Laidlaw: preserves its demanding defendant-cessation burden while distinguishing this claimant's abandonment of its own regulated activity.
- U.S. Bancorp Mortgage Co. v. Bonner Mall Partnership: applies equitable rather than automatic vacatur after voluntary loss of review; no exhaustive list of exceptional equities or new proof quantum is adopted.
- Freedman v. Maryland and FW/PBS, Inc. v. Dallas: their prompt-review requirements are not construed because this applicant's case is moot; the Wisconsin court's interpretation is not affirmed.

### Review of a mandatory approval condition

#### Urofsky v. Gilmore

**Docket or dockets:** Fourth Circuit No. 98-1481; no separate Supreme Court docket supplied; judgment below, 216 F.3d 401
**Decided:** February 20, 2001.
