**Operative remedy or transition:** Reverse the categorical Governor dismissal, 5–4, without finding bad faith or granting a compact, mediator, default proposal, Secretary procedures, damages, game-scope declaration or license. The separate September 22, 1993 merits judgment and stayed appeal No. 93-5256 receive their own lawful treatment and are not vacated. The asserted Secretary substitute remedy, independent administrative authority, complete state-law compacting power, Tenth Amendment defense and mediator-appointments question remain unadjudicated.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Young reaches this connected officer and ongoing federal duty without a universal rule for detailed statutes. Edelman's retrospective-compensation boundary remains; prospective costs alone do not establish retrospective relief. Pennhurst is not triggered merely because the federal obligation incorporates state gaming law. Hafer's personal-capacity damages rule does not supply this action. Puerto Rico Aqueduct supplies appealability, not immunity. New York and Voting Rights Coalition do not decide the reserved IGRA constitutional defenses; X-Citement Video preserves appropriate respondent defenses without compelling their resolution here.

##### State amenability lacks a controlling rationale, despite agreement on direct-State statutory intent

**Authority of the judgment:** Florida's immunity-dismissal direction is set aside and remanded by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer, 5–4. Stone-Zsela and Stevens would sustain direct-State amenability; Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer would vacate without deciding it. O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas would affirm. No controlling State-amenability rationale exists under Marks: avoidance is not a logical subset of the affirmative constitutional grounds, and three unused conditional alternatives are not actual abrogation joins.

**Independently majority-supported proposition:** IGRA unmistakably authorizes an action naming the State through its State-party action, evidentiary burden, State-and-Tribe order and successive State-directed steps. General federal-question jurisdiction, §1362 or scattered State references alone would not suffice.

**Proposition-level authority:** Six actual positions across the separate writings: Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas. The other three's conditional acceptance is not counted. This agreement concerns statutory intent alone.

**Current force:** The clear-statement proposition controls; no majority sustains or rejects Florida's constitutional amenability or requires a particular avoidance sequence.

**Later-authority backlinks:** Florida Prepaid Postsecondary Education Expense Board v. College Savings Bank, June 23, 1999, Part III, resolves the Article I question left open here: Congress may authorize private enforcement against States when the underlying obligation and enforcement measure fall within its Article I authority and Congress unmistakably authorizes the State's exposure to suit. It extends Union Gas's retained constitutional conclusion and does not decide IGRA's direct-State action; see Federal Courts — Congress unmistakably authorized this federal patent action against States.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The State-removal judgment establishes neither jurisdiction nor its absence; lawful jurisdiction is necessary before any State merits adjudication. The State claim is not terminated by immunity, and no abandonment, mootness or indefinite stay is presumed. No express or constructive waiver, reciprocal tribal/state consent or delegated United States immunity exemption establishes this suit. The United States appears only as amicus.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Union Gas is not overruled: its damages judgment, statutory holding and five-vote constitutional conclusion retain their decided force without making every plurality sentence binding. Hans remains effective; separate common-law theories do not become law. Fitzpatrick's distinct Fourteenth Amendment authority is unchanged.

### Prison access and prospective relief

#### Lewis v. Casey, 518 U.S. 343 (1996)

**Docket or dockets:** No. 94-1511.
**Decided:** June 24, 1996.

##### Whether books alone satisfy the right of meaningful access

**Holding:** Bounds requires a meaningful practical opportunity for court access through effective libraries, trained assistance or other effective arrangements considered as a whole; adequate books do not discharge that duty for prisoners unable to use them without needed assistance. It guarantees neither a preferred library program, physical browsing in every case, professional counsel for every prisoner nor the decree's numerical requirements.

**Proposition-level authority:** Kennedy’s Part I, unanimous.

**Material application:** Bartholic’s dismissal and Harris’s inability to file establish actual obstruction at their supported scope. An inventory-based paging system with prompt delivery may work for prisoners capable of independent use; that conditional possibility proves neither Arizona’s actual adequacy nor adequacy for differing literacy or language needs.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No fixed number of all injured prisoners, right to ultimate success, constitutional library specification or automatic appointed counsel is established.

##### What connection to a legal claim is required for access liability

**Holding:** A Bounds access-liability claim requires actual or evidence-supported imminent hindrance of a qualifying, nonfrivolous legal claim, subject to the validity of restrictions reasonably related to legitimate penological interests. The prisoner need not win the obstructed case or complete an impossible filing, but a deficient inventory, preferred staffing arrangement or abstract wish for better research does not alone establish the violation.

**Proposition-level authority:** Kennedy’s Part II, joined by Stone-Zsela, O’Connor, Scalia and Thomas; five Justices.

**Material application:** Technical dismissal, inability to file a contemplated qualifying claim or sufficiently concrete threatened obstruction can supply the connection. A procedural defect does not itself make the underlying claim substantively frivolous. Evidence and reasonable inferences may prove the real claim and obstacle before avoidable filing failure.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The additional Article III preliminary-merits screen, subgroup-standing requirement and categorical sentence/conditions and initial-filing restrictions have only four votes. Ordinary Lujan injury, causation and stage-appropriate proof remain. Genuine state-created-liberty claims remain possible under Sandin; no categorical exclusion of every other retained right, universal end of assistance upon filing or general private-litigation subsidy is adopted. Independent communications and petition claims retain their own predicates.

##### What proof and governmental role support classwide prison-access relief

**Holding:** Common relief must correspond to a demonstrated common barrier and an identified affected group; individual obstruction or a resource deficiency alone does not establish an unrestricted statewide violation. Relief must respect restrictions reasonably related to legitimate penological interests and give the State a genuine opportunity to devise an effective cure, while permitting measures actually necessary to repair the proved wrong.

**Proposition-level authority:** Kennedy’s Part III, joined by Stone-Zsela, O’Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer; eight Justices.

**Material application:** Common evidence may prove a common barrier without proof that every class member has already lost a suit, but geographical and functional reach require support. The actual findings do not establish a denial at every facility or independent broad entitlements for all non-English-speaking or lockdown prisoners. Opportunity to rebut a presumptive Gluth statewide template did not supply Arizona’s genuine initial remedial design role.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Turner requires examination of the actual reasonable relationship to legitimate penological interests with appropriate deference; injury alone does not invalidate a valid restriction, and merely asserting security does not establish validity. Jenkins permits necessary repair measures without making every remedial detail a freestanding constitutional right.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Unsupported classwide approval set aside and remanded, 8–1; Stevens accepts a narrower remedial remand. Established obstruction, supported findings and common evidence remain. No unrestricted statewide violation, automatic dismissal or fixed library, telephone, staffing or resource minimum.

##### How original PLRA §802 governs this existing prospective decree and any replacement

**Holding:** Section 802 applies amended section 3626 to prospective relief granted before, on or after April 26, 1996; relief here requires findings that it is narrowly drawn, extends no further than necessary to correct the federal-right violation of the particular plaintiffs, and is the least intrusive means necessary, with substantial weight given to adverse effects on public safety or criminal-justice-system operation. Relief exceeding official state-law authority or otherwise violating state or local law additionally requires federal permission, necessity to correct the federal violation, and absence of any other corrective relief.

**Proposition-level authority:** Kennedy’s Part IV, joined by all eight Associate Justices; Stone-Zsela agrees only with the statutory remand disposition.

**Material application:** Amended §3626 applies to relief granted before, on or after April 26, 1996. Prospective relief means relief other than compensatory monetary damages. This prison-conditions action is not excluded habeas merely because access assistance facilitates habeas. Existing findings may satisfy actual predicates without ritual wording; reasonable cost or absence of hardship does not substitute for need, narrowness and least intrusiveness.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Section 3626(a)(1)(B)’s federal-permission, necessity and no-other-relief conditions govern state/local-law conflicts separately from (A). Subsection (C) supplies no authority to build prisons or raise taxes and preserves existing limits. New preliminary relief must be otherwise authorized and satisfy narrowness, need, least intrusiveness, safety and the separate state-law terms; it expires ninety days after entry unless the required prospective findings are made and the order made final before expiration. The former permanent decree is not automatically a new preliminary order. No constitutional challenge to §802 or prisoner-release remedy is decided.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Prospective-decree approval set aside and remanded, 9–0 in disposition, eight on statutory reasoning. Actual findings, alternatives, improvements and security must be considered; the decree is neither automatically retained nor dissolved.

**Later-authority backlinks:** Plyler v. Moore, Fourth Circuit No. 96-6884, 1997-06-23, resolves the reserved Article III challenge to §3626(b)(2) only with the original (b)(3) current-or-ongoing safeguard; see Constitutional Structure — Congressional amendment of law governing prospective relief. Pub. L. 105-119, §123(a)(1)(A), (b), effective 1997-11-26, changes the federal-law condition for state/local-law-conflicting prospective relief and applies it to pending cases.

**Current-force modification:** Effective November 26, 1997, including pending cases, Pub. L. 105-119, §123(a)(1)(A), 111 Stat. 2470, changes §3626(a)(1)(B)(i) from federal permission to federal requirement: the relief that exceeds state/local authority or violates state/local law must be required by federal law, with the separate necessity and no-other-corrective-relief conditions retained. The announced Holding states the original text and remains its construction; the amendment supplies the later statutory condition without altering the prior adjudication.

##### When termination and an automatic stay arise under the original §3626

**Holding:** The original section 3626 termination provisions do not dissolve relief merely upon enactment: the immediate-termination entitlement for relief entered without the required findings remains subject to written, record-based findings that relief is necessary to correct a current OR ongoing federal-right violation and remains narrowly drawn, no broader than necessary, and least intrusive. The automatic stay requires an actual pending qualifying motion and runs only for the original statutory period described below.

**Proposition-level authority:** Kennedy’s Part IV, joined by all eight Associate Justices; Stone-Zsela agrees only with the statutory remand disposition.

**Material application:** Under (b)(1), a party or intervener may seek termination after the applicable two years from relief, one year after denial under that paragraph, or two years after enactment for preenactment relief; earlier agreement is permitted. Under (b)(2), a defendant or intervener is entitled to immediate termination absent the original required findings, subject to (b)(3)’s written record findings of necessary, narrowly drawn, no-broader-than-necessary and least-intrusive relief for a current OR ongoing violation. Subsection (b)(4) preserves earlier relief available under other law.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Modification/termination motions must be decided promptly. Original (e) stays the challenged relief from day thirty after a pending (b)(1)/(b)(2) motion, or day 180 for a motion under other law, until the final order resolving it. No such filing is established. Consent decrees remain subject to (a); private agreements enforceable federally only by reinstating the settled proceeding are distinct, with available state breach remedies preserved. Relief entered by state courts solely on state claims is outside the statute.

**Operative remedy or transition:** No termination or automatic stay is entered by enactment or this remand.

**Current-force modification:** Effective November 26, 1997, including pending cases, Pub. L. 105-119, §123(a)(2)–(3), (b), 111 Stat. 2470–2471, replaces §3626(b)(3)’s current-or-ongoing condition with current and ongoing, retaining the written record findings and need, narrowness and least-intrusiveness requirements. The amended §3626(e)(1) provides that mandamus shall lie to remedy any failure to issue a prompt ruling; (e)(2) makes a motion to modify or terminate prospective relief under (b) operate as the prescribed stay, retaining the statutory thirty-day and 180-day timing branches and final-order endpoint. New (e)(3) permits postponing the stay’s effective date by no more than sixty days for good cause, excluding general calendar congestion. Under new (e)(4), orders staying, suspending, delaying or barring that stay, apart from the (e)(3) postponement, are appealable under §1292(a)(1) as orders refusing to dissolve or modify an injunction regardless of their name or preliminary/final designation. The original-text holding remains unchanged for its earlier operation; the enactment supplies no individual motion, stay or deadline and resolves no constitutional challenge.

**Later-authority backlinks:** Pub. L. 105-119, §123(a)(2)–(3), (b), effective 1997-11-26, pending-case termination-preservation and stay amendments. Inmates of Suffolk County Jail v. Rouse, First Circuit Nos. 97-1261, 97-1262, 97-1263 and 97-1334, decided 1998-01-15, applies the amended preservation condition where no violation qualifying only under the broader original condition is identified.

##### What conditions govern continued special-master assistance

**Holding:** A special master in this prison-conditions remedy must be disinterested and objective, give due regard to public safety, and be warranted by a finding of sufficient remedial complexity; appointments, compensation, work and continued need must comply with section 3626(f)'s separate conditions below. Those requirements apply to the master's statutory role regardless of title and do not themselves establish misconduct or invalidate every prior act or accrued fee.

**Proposition-level authority:** Kennedy’s Part IV, joined by all eight Associate Justices; Stone-Zsela agrees only with the statutory remand disposition.

**Material application:** A necessary appointment under (f) requires each side to submit up to five names, each may strike up to three opposing names, and the court chooses among those remaining; the prescribed partiality interlocutory appeal remains. Compensation is capped at the §3006A appointed-counsel hourly rate plus reasonable costs, paid from Judiciary appropriations. Continuing need must be reviewed every six months. Hearings and proposed findings are on the record; ex parte findings and communications are forbidden. A master may assist remedial plans and be removed at any time, and service ends upon termination of relief. Title alone cannot evade the statutory role.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The prior opportunity to challenge fees survives. Accrued-fee and temporal questions remain for adjudication; no misconduct, retrospective invalidity of every prior act, repayment or automatic new appointment is established.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- **Bounds v. Smith, 430 U.S. 817, and Wolff v. McDonnell, 418 U.S. 539:** Preserve meaningful access and effective methods rather than a constitutional library specification. Bounds is not overruled; four proposed categorical limits do not become Court law.
- **Murray v. Giarratano, 492 U.S. 1:** Preserves choice among effective assistance arrangements without a general right to appointed counsel for every prisoner.
- **Lujan v. Defenders of Wildlife, June 12, 1992, and City of Los Angeles v. Lyons, 461 U.S. 95:** Preserve personal injury, causation, appropriate prospective exposure and proof at the litigation stage. The Court does not adopt the four-Justice preliminary merits screen or decide standing in gross from a party concession.
- **Turner v. Safley, 482 U.S. 78:** Applies reasonable relationship to legitimate penological interests, with actual examination and proper deference rather than automatic acceptance of a security assertion.
- **Missouri v. Jenkins, June 12, 1995:** Applies violation-linked repair while preserving implementation measures necessary to correct a proved wrong. Each means need not independently violate the Constitution.
- **Rufo v. Inmates of Suffolk County Jail, 502 U.S. 367:** Preserves lawful responsive modification; the new statute's distinct mandatory requirements must also be applied.
- **Sandin v. Conner, June 19, 1995:** Preserves its actual rule for meaningful state-created prison liberty under genuine substantive constraints. No categorical hardship replacement is imported to exclude access to those claims.
- **Madsen v. Women's Health Center, Inc., June 30, 1994:** Preserves connection between remedial terms and their predicate; its speech-specific standard is not a universal least-restrictive-means rule or a substitute for section 3626.

No precedent is overruled. Gluth's earlier remedy supplies background and a proposed template, not a finding of statewide injury in this record.

#### Plyler v. Moore

**Docket or dockets:** Fourth Circuit No. 96-6884; no separate Supreme Court docket assigned.

**Decided:** 1997-06-23.

For the Article III validity of the conditional termination rule, see Plyler v. Moore, Fourth Circuit No. 96-6884, decided 1997-06-23, Constitutional Structure — Congressional amendment of law governing prospective relief.

##### I. The original PLRA supplies an independent, conditional termination rule

**Holding:** For covered federal prison-conditions prospective relief, including relief entered before April 26, 1996, original §3626(b)(2) entitles a defendant or intervener to immediate termination when the relief was approved without findings that it was narrowly drawn, extended no further than necessary to correct the federal-right violation and was the least intrusive necessary means. Under original §3626(b)(3), relief must nevertheless continue upon written, record-based findings that it remains necessary to correct a current **or** ongoing federal violation, is no broader than necessary, and is narrowly drawn and the least intrusive means; existing substantive findings may satisfy the statute without ritual wording.

**Proposition-level authority:** Kennedy's opinion of the Court is joined in full by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer. All nine directly adopt the following rules and qualifications.

**Material application:** Section 802 expressly reaches existing prospective relief. The State must establish the termination basis and the court must independently apply (b)(3); missing ritual phrases, age of the decree or relief exceeding constitutional minima alone does not establish dispensability.

**Limits and questions not reached:** This is (b)(2) termination, not the waiting-period route under (b)(1) or automatic stay under (e). The legal appeal establishes neither a preserved failure to consider qualifying findings nor a supported violation requiring a new hearing; no statewide compliance finding or accrued monetary liability is decided.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Lewis supplies the complete original findings and preservation conditions. Rufo remains the equitable modification rule, but its significant-change threshold is no additional prerequisite to this independent statutory route.

**Current-force modification:** From November 26, 1997, including pending cases, Pub. L. 105-119, §123(a)(2), (b), 111 Stat. 2470–2471, requires a current and ongoing federal-right violation under §3626(b)(3), instead of the original current-or-ongoing condition. The independent (b)(2) route and the substantive written-findings, need, narrowness and least-intrusiveness requirements remain. The announced Holding continues to state the original-text rule; the amendment does not rewrite that adjudication.

**Later-authority backlinks:** Pub. L. 105-119, §123(a)(2), (b), effective 1997-11-26, changes the preservation condition for pending cases. Inmates of Suffolk County Jail v. Rouse, First Circuit Nos. 97-1261, 97-1262, 97-1263 and 97-1334, decided 1998-01-15, applies the amended condition without deciding a case where the changed conjunction excludes an otherwise supported violation.

##### III. Negotiated decree protections are not automatically independent federal rights

**Holding:** Incorporating agreed protections into a federal consent decree does not make every protection exceeding underlying federal law the independent federal right whose violation prevents termination under §3626(b)(2). Continuing prospective enforcement remains subject to the statute, while relief necessary to correct an actual federal violation must be preserved under the full original (b)(3) conditions.

**Proposition-level authority:** Kennedy's opinion of the Court is joined in full by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer. All nine directly adopt the following rules and qualifications.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Relief exceeding a constitutional minimum may still be necessary to correct an actual federal violation. Separate contractual or state-law claims and independently accrued rights are not adjudicated.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Rufo permits negotiated obligations beyond constitutional minima without insulating their future enforcement from valid legislation. Lewis permits necessary corrective measures; Joseph A.'s performance-condition ruling, made without a PLRA prison-conditions predicate, does not displace this statutory route.

**Current-force modification:** The distinction between negotiated decree terms and independent federal rights remains controlling. For the preservation condition incorporated in this proposition, Pub. L. 105-119, §123(a)(2), (b), effective November 26, 1997 and applicable to pending cases, substitutes current and ongoing for current or ongoing; the other preservation requirements remain. The full statutory transition appears in this case’s independent-termination question above.

**Later-authority backlinks:** Pub. L. 105-119, §123(a)(2), (b), effective 1997-11-26, changes the incorporated preservation condition; Inmates of Suffolk County Jail v. Rouse, decided 1998-01-15, applies the amended condition and preserves the decree/right distinction.

##### IV. The presented due-process claim does not bar termination

**Holding:** Beneficiaries of this mutable prospective decree have no vested entitlement to perpetual federal enforcement of its unchanged terms despite valid later law. On the presented legal challenge, the judicial motion process, opportunity to oppose termination and appellate review satisfy due process; no additional evidentiary remand follows from an unestablished possibility that a statutory preservation condition was overlooked.

**Proposition-level authority:** Kennedy's opinion of the Court is joined in full by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer. All nine directly adopt the following rules and qualifications.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No unrecorded hearing is presumed or actual denial of an opportunity to present a preserved factual claim adjudicated. A demonstrated failure to apply (b)(3) would be distinct, and state sovereignty would not excuse it. General equal protection, accrued or contractual rights, other PLRA provisions and particular current prison conditions remain undecided.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Termination of prospective federal enforcement is affirmed unanimously against the presented legal objections. No new hearing, automatic stay, prisoner-release order, accrued-damages cancellation or extinguishment of independent rights follows.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Lewis's meaningful-access and original current-or-ongoing protections remain operative.

#### Inmates of Suffolk County Jail v. Rouse

**Docket or dockets:** First Circuit Nos. 97-1261, 97-1262, 97-1263 and 97-1334; no separate Supreme Court docket stated.

**Decided:** 1998-01-15.

For the Article III validity of the prospective-law change, see Inmates of Suffolk County Jail v. Rouse, First Circuit Nos. 97-1261, 97-1262, 97-1263 and 97-1334, decided January 15, 1998, Constitutional Structure — Congressional amendment of law governing prospective relief.

##### The independent statutory termination route and amended preservation condition govern

**Holding:** Section 3626(b)(2) permits termination of covered prospective prison-conditions relief approved without the substantive need, narrowness and least-intrusiveness findings. The State must establish that predicate. Existing actual findings can suffice without ritual statutory wording, and Rufo's separate equitable significant-change threshold is not an additional prerequisite. Under §3626(b)(3), as amended effective November 26, 1997 and applicable to pending cases, preservation requires written, record-based findings of necessity to correct a current and ongoing federal-right violation, with relief no broader than necessary, narrowly drawn and least intrusive. Consent decrees and pretrial-detention conditions are covered. Negotiated terms are not automatically independent federal rights, but necessary corrective relief may exceed the minimum conduct rule.

**Proposition-level authority:** Kennedy's Part I, unanimous.

##### The supported findings assessment permits termination without a speculative new inquiry

**Holding:** The reported judicial evaluation establishes no qualifying findings or supported federal violation requiring preservation of this relief. No violation meeting the broader original condition but excluded by the amended conjunction is identified. The narrower text therefore creates no outcome-determinative difference on this record.

**Proposition-level authority:** Kennedy's Part I, unanimous.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Independent judicial review and a proper opportunity to offer relevant evidence remain required. Evaluated reports and existing findings support termination here; no qualifying violation under the broader original condition is identified. No permanent compliance finding or new hearing follows from speculative deterioration.


##### Vested-right and procedural due process do not preserve unchanged future enforcement

**Holding:** Beneficiaries possess no perpetual entitlement to federal enforcement of the mutable decree's unchanged prospective terms against valid later remedial law. The reported motion, opposition and review support rejection of the presented procedural objection.

**Proposition-level authority:** Kennedy's Part III, unanimous.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Past lawful effects and independently accrued claims are preserved. Calling continuing judicial obligations contractual does not exempt them from the governing termination conditions.

##### The assumed independent contract-impairment objection fails at its presented scope

**Holding:** Assuming a contract and substantial impairment solely to decide the objection, the federal-nonparty rationality framework permits this restriction on continuing federal supervision. The United States does not become a contracting party merely because a federal court approved the decree or Congress enacted legislation benefiting States.

**Proposition-level authority:** Kennedy's Part IV has all eight Associates; Stone-Zsela agrees only in rejecting the objection.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Neither a separate contract’s existence, survival, breach or enforceability nor accrued contractual liability is decided.

##### The presented equality challenge fails without diminishing pretrial rights or meaningful access

**Holding:** Ordinary rational review permits the statute's restriction on continuing prison-decree supervision because it reasonably relates the actual supervisory disadvantage to legitimate institutional responsibility and resource concerns while retaining enforceable federal-right protection.

**Proposition-level authority:** Kennedy's Part III, unanimous.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Pretrial protection under Bell and meaningful court access remain; nonsuspect status alone does not prove validity, and no blanket prohibition on supported threatened-injury relief is adopted.

##### Termination ends prospective judicial operation, not past adjudication

**Holding:** The statutory direction to terminate, together with the inclusion of consent decrees in prospective relief, requires ending the decree's continuing judicial obligations themselves. It does not declare the original judgment erroneous, erase completed determinations or payments, or retrospectively vacate lawful past effects.

**Proposition-level authority:** All nine join the prospective/historical distinction; Kennedy's Part V ordinary-affirmance mandate has all eight Associates.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The separate Rule 60(b) theory was set aside conditionally upon obtaining termination; its merits remain unaddressed once that condition is satisfied.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The First Circuit judgment is affirmed, including termination of the decree’s future operation without retrospective annulment. No new merits inquiry, hearing, release, automatic stay or deadline is ordered.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

Plyler supplies the independent statutory route, judicial preservation inquiry and bounded constitutional operation under its original text; the present amendment is applied without rewriting that holding. Lewis retains the statute's scope, transition, findings and meaningful-access limits. Rufo retains its independent significant-change and tailored-modification rule, not an extra statutory prerequisite. Morgan Stanley and Plaut retain their limited completed-dismissal reinstatement holdings; Robertson, Klein and Wheeling Bridge preserve the changed-law/adjudication distinction. Bell preserves pretrial protection. Beach, Heller and Romer supply ordinary review within their actual limits, and Atchison supplies the assumed federal-nonparty contract analysis. No precedent is overruled.

### State immunity and collateral indemnification

#### Regents of the University of California v. Doe

**Docket or dockets:** No. 95-1694.
**Decided:** February 19, 1997.

##### Collateral indemnification does not alter an assumed state arm's immunity

**Holding:** A third party's contractual promise to reimburse litigation expense or an adverse judgment does not by itself divest an otherwise immune state instrumentality of Eleventh Amendment immunity. The instrumentality remains the defendant and primary legal obligor; collateral reimbursement does not replace the complete inquiry into its legal, financial and institutional relationship with the State or make structural financing irrelevant to antecedent arm status.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Stone-Zsela), unanimous.

**Material application:** The University remains the defendant and primary obligor; DOE's reimbursement undertaking does not substitute a federal defendant or extinguish that obligation. Initial state-arm status is assumed for the indemnity question.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Structural financing remains relevant to antecedent arm status. Independently open arm-status, waiver, abrogation, prospective or individual-defendant claims and contract merits remain unresolved.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Reversal and remand remove indemnification as a basis for setting aside the district immunity disposition on the accepted-arm premise; they do not newly dismiss every claim or order employment or damages.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Hess's whole-entity inquiry is applied to collateral reimbursement, preserving highly probative but nonexclusive treasury exposure. Mt. Healthy and Lake Country Estates continue to distinguish state arms from independently structured public bodies.

### State immunity and local public bodies

#### Auer v. Robbins

**Docket or dockets:** No. 95-897.
**Decided:** February 19, 1997.

For the separate questions in Auer v. Robbins, No. 95-897 decided February 19, 1997, see Administrative Law — Interpretation of agency regulations (The Secretary's fair and considered regulatory interpretation controls within its legal bounds); Labor and Employment — Public-employee salary-basis exemptions (The salary-basis rule validly implements the public-employee exemption; Practical exposure requires an actual practice or an effective particular policy; Correction eligibility requires actual reimbursement and future compliance).

##### The Board's local relationship does not make it an arm of Missouri

**Holding:** The Board is not an arm of Missouri entitled to the State's immunity in these actions because its combined financial, budgetary and operational relationship is local. State creation, statutory regulation and gubernatorial appointments do not alone make a separate public body the State; treasury exposure carries substantial weight alongside institutional control rather than serving as an indispensable sole condition.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part I of Kennedy's opinion, joined by all seven other Associate Justices; eight rationale votes. Stone-Zsela agrees with the threshold result only.

**Material application:** State creation and regulation and the governor's four appointments matter, but municipal financing, budget and appropriation control, city-approval limits and local operations establish the Board's combined local relationship.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Court does not decide waiver by litigation conduct, valid abrogation, a retrospective officer remedy or the State's own amenability.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Hess and Mt. Healthy require the entity-specific institutional inquiry, with treasury exposure important but not exclusive. Seminole Tribe is not expanded into a categorical Article I answer.

### Consent-decree termination and reviewable findings

#### New Mexico Department of Human Services v. Joseph A. and Josephine A.

**Docket or dockets:** Tenth Circuit No. 94-2160; no separate Supreme Court docket supplied. 69 F.3d 1081 identifies the judgment below.
**Decided:** April 21, 1997.

##### What performance entitles the Department to termination of this foster-care decree

**Holding:** Termination requires twelve consecutive months of substantial and continuous compliance with the decree's essential purposes and stated obligations. Immaterial deviations need not prevent release, but general progress cannot excuse material failure in fair, reasonable, and timely adoption decisions or adequate procedures securing access to permanent homes.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Thomas), unanimous.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The required procedures must actually be established, without perfect compliance, a percentage safe harbor, an indefinite assurance against future lapses, or an added period beyond the agreed twelve months.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** *ITT Continental Baking* requires interpretation of the agreed obligations in light of their purposes, preserving both the beneficiaries' rights and the Department's right to release upon the actual condition.

##### What findings Rule 52 requires to support termination

**Holding:** The District Court must state enough subsidiary findings and explain material evidence and methodology to permit review of whether the termination condition is met. Rule 52(a) governs adopted master findings; a long evidentiary summary or repeated ultimate conclusion is insufficient, although irrelevant details and every item need not be recited.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Thomas), unanimous.

**Material application:** The reports did not connect wide-ranging conference and content measures to their compliance conclusions, explain the attorney-notification shortfalls, justify discounting adverse individual cases, resolve the timely ADAPT-data challenge, or explain or discredit the custody-duration evidence. Those gaps preclude review without establishing actual noncompliance.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Supported findings retain clear-error protection. The appellate court may not invent a methodology, decide credibility, or weigh conflicts for the trial tribunal. Both adopted reports are within review of final termination; the 1989 report was already inadequate alone. The later Auditor report and proposed study not presented to the Master supply no adverse finding, distinct from timely quality-assurance evidence considered below.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Affirm vacatur of termination and remand for adequate findings. No Supreme Court finding of actual compliance or systemic noncompliance follows from the cited disputed measures.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** *Kelley v. Everglades Drainage District* requires findings that disclose the ultimate conclusion's factual basis.

##### Whether performance-based release requires changed circumstances or permits a new constitutional-minimum decree

**Holding:** Proving the decree's existing termination condition does not require a separate significant-change showing under *Rufo*. A request to reduce continuing obligations because law or facts changed does require *Rufo*'s changed-circumstances and tailoring analysis; ordinary compliance review cannot replace the decree with a constitutional minimum.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Thomas), unanimous.

**Limits and questions not reached:** No new constitutional-violation prerequisite for enforcement or PLRA prison-conditions predicate is established. School-specific release requirements do not replace this foster-care decree's agreed standard.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** *Rufo* governs modification of existing duties, distinguished from proof of their performance. *Freeman* and *Missouri v. Jenkins* retain their school-desegregation rules and illustrate findings tied to the operative remedy without supplying a substitute checklist here.

##### What legal effect the findings remand has on the decree and release condition

**Holding:** The decree remains effective until lawfully terminated or modified. The District Court must explain material deviations and methodology and resolve necessary factual conflicts, then grant termination if supported findings establish the existing twelve-month condition.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Thomas), unanimous.

**Operative remedy or transition:** The existing remand may proceed on the record or through additional proceedings authorized by ordinary law. No new twelve-month clock, fixed release date, required plaintiff consent, consensus, neutral-reviewer endorsement, new monitor, automatic retrial of settled facts, or automatic admission of submissions is imposed.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The separate monitoring-fee appeal, including prevailing-party status, necessity of work, and fee-amount findings, is outside review. The ruling establishes no general federal foster-care supervisory mandate.

### Presidential immunity appeals and trial stays

#### Clinton v. Jones, 520 U.S. 681 (1997)

**Docket or dockets:** No. 95-1853.
**Decided:** 1997-05-27.

For the constitutional immunity and accommodation rules, see Constitutional Structure — Presidential civil immunity and accommodation, Clinton v. Jones, May 27, 1997, No. 95-1853.

##### Immediate review reaches this integrated immunity and trial-protection order

**Holding:** An appeal from the conclusive rejection of the President's asserted immunity from present suit permits review of this same order's categorical trial protection, including its alternative docket-control basis, because those rulings determine the scope and remedy of the same asserted temporal immunity. It does not permit review of independent liability claims or defenses, and creates no automatic appeal from a later scheduling order.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; eight direct rationale votes. Breyer concurs in the judgment only.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Common facts, efficiency or presidential importance alone do not authorize connected review. Independent liability, official-defamation merits, general pendent-party jurisdiction and future scheduling/process orders remain outside this appeal; the completed lower-appeal discovery-stay dispute is moot.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Fitzgerald permits immediate review of asserted immunity from present suit. Swint's necessary/inextricable-review reservation is resolved only for this integrated temporal-protection order; unrelated nonappealable liability questions remain excluded.

##### This categorical trial stay is an abuse of discretion

**Holding:** The District Court abused its discretion by ordering in advance that trial await the end of the Presidency without a developed showing connecting that duration to particular presidential duties and without sufficiently accounting for the plaintiff's timely-adjudication and evidence interests. Ordinary stay authority survives the rejection of constitutional immunity, but generalized burdens and the duration of office do not justify this order.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stone-Zsela, Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg; eight direct rationale votes. Breyer concurs in the judgment only.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Continuing discovery does not remove all trial-delay prejudice. No actual evidentiary loss or disputed survival-law finding is necessary; a later supported request remains possible.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Affirm removal of the Presidency-long stay and Ferguson's exclusively derivative stay, 9–0, without deciding independent Ferguson defenses. No trial date, compelled appearance or particular production is ordered.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Landis retains ordinary stay power, a clear showing of hardship or inequity when even a fair possibility of harm to the other party exists, and bounded, moderate, nonoppressive duration. A term endpoint is no justification for the entire delay.

### State appellate procedure and federal immunity

#### Johnson v. Fankell

**Docket or dockets:** No. 96-292.

**Decided:** 1997-06-09.

##### Federal immunity does not require a state interlocutory appeal under a neutral finality rule

**Holding:** Neither §1983, the Supremacy Clause, nor federal qualified-immunity doctrine requires a State to provide an interlocutory appeal from denial of immunity when its neutral final-judgment rule preserves meaningful adjudication and review of the substantive defense. Section 1291's federal collateral-order appeal is not itself a substantive component that state appellate courts must adopt; discriminatory or substantively destructive state procedures present a different question.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's opinion of the Court, unanimous.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The defense against trial burdens retains substantive force. Discriminatory procedures, deliberate evasion, denial of meaningful review or destruction of federal protection remain distinct. Idaho Rule 12(a) permits interlocutory review of a controlling legal question with substantial grounds for disagreement when immediate review may materially advance orderly resolution, but neither an application nor permission is found and the holding does not depend on that route.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Affirm dismissal of the interlocutory appeal. State proceedings continue with the federal substantive defense and ordinary final review available; no immunity entitlement, liability, damages, stay or new immediate appeal is adjudicated.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- **Mitchell v. Forsyth:** Its qualifying immediate federal immunity appeal remains a construction of §1291; it does not independently impose state appellate jurisdiction.
- **Johnson v. Jones:** Its separation of legal immunity questions from factual-sufficiency disputes illustrates that substantive immunity does not create unrestricted immediate review.
- **Swint v. Chambers County Commission:** Its limits on collateral appellate jurisdiction remain effective; an available appeal does not automatically carry unrelated nonappealable issues.
- **Behrens v. Pelletier:** Its authorization of qualifying successive-stage federal appeals remains subject to the federal statute and cumulative jurisdictional limits; it does not bind a State to that appellate structure.
- **Howlett v. Rose:** Applied to require state-court fidelity to federal substantive law and to forbid discrimination or nullification, without imposing an identical federal appellate system.
- **Testa v. Katt:** Its obligation to respect federal law is preserved; neutral judicial administration is not rejected merely because it differs from federal procedure.
- **Felder v. Casey:** Distinguished because the incompatible notice condition there defeated the federal claim, unlike the neutral timing rule presented here.
- **Dice v. Akron, Canton & Youngstown Railroad Co.:** Distinguished because the relevant procedure came from the substantive federal statute; no equivalent source mandates this state appeal.

### Modification of continuing injunctions

#### Agostini v. Felton; Chancellor, Board of Education of the City of New York v. Felton

**Docket or dockets:** Nos. 96-552 and 96-553.
**Decided:** June 23, 1997.

**Material judgment:** Both judgments vacated and remanded, 5–4, for tailored prospective Rule 60(b)(5) relief; Lemon modified as the governing test; Aguilar's categorical rule overruled and Ball's corresponding Shared Time rationale displaced at the stated scope.

**Proposition-level authority:** All four holdings are delivered by Chief Justice Stone-Zsela in the Opinion of the Court, joined in full by Justices O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy and Thomas. Each has five direct joins at its stated level of generality, including the modification of Lemon in Holding I. No aggregation of separate opinions supplies controlling authority.

##### III. The Court's present change supplies the legal change required for prospective relief

**Holding:** When this Court expressly removes the constitutional premise supporting a continuing adjudicated injunction, that present change is a significant change in law under Rule 60(b)(5), and continued categorical enforcement on the displaced premise is inequitable. The Court may apply its ruling in the pending review of denied prospective relief without requiring a second motion merely to invoke the rule it has just announced; lower courts remain bound by directly controlling precedent until this Court changes it.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part III of the Court's opinion; the same five Justices directly join the present-change rule and its distinction between this continuing decree and a completed judgment.

**Controlling explanation:** Denial of Rule 60(b)(5) relief is reviewed for abuse of discretion, with its governing legal premises reviewed as questions of law. Rufo requires a significant change making continued enforcement inequitable and a modification fitted to that change. The legal change here is the Court's ruling today. It is not the expectation that the Court might reconsider Aguilar, an increase in the expense of compliance, or a supposed earlier overruling. Kiryas Joel expressly preserved Aguilar and Ball. Zobrest decided antecedent statutory and regulatory questions without deciding establishment. Rosenberger's actual printing benefit and Pinette and Chabad's private-expression holdings did not authorize this public teaching program. Rodriguez de Quijas required the lower courts to apply the directly governing rule.

The objection that Rule 60 review cannot become an untimely rehearing of the original judgment does not bar this relief. The motion addresses the decree's continuing prospective operation, and the preserved question presents the constitutional rule supporting that operation. The Court changes that rule expressly; it does not hold that the district court should have anticipated the change. Requiring a new motion solely to invoke today's decision would preserve a now-rejected prohibition without a remaining legal reason.

Rufo's limits remain. Dissatisfaction, a cheaper lawful alternative and anticipated doctrinal change are insufficient. Actual anticipation and acceptance of a risk can impose a heavy burden, but mere foreseeability is not an absolute bar and an unforeseeable catastrophe is unnecessary. A clarification of constitutional permission does not automatically erase a voluntarily accepted stricter consent obligation. This injunction enforces an adjudicated constitutional prohibition; the record establishes no negotiated promise independently requiring that prohibition. The Court neither reopens completed claims nor finds an independently sufficient factual change in costs.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Rufo v. Inmates of Suffolk County Jail is applied to the present legal change and retained in its significant-change, accepted-risk and tailored-relief requirements. Rodriguez de Quijas v. Shearson/American Express, Inc. is applied to preserve lower-court obedience to direct precedent. Browder v. Director, Department of Corrections does not foreclose adjustment of continuing relief on the basis of the legal change actually adopted here; its limit on using a Rule 60 appeal to reopen the underlying completed judgment remains.

##### IV. Relief must remove the rejected prohibition while preserving independently lawful obligations

**Holding:** On remand the district court must remove the injunction's prohibition of the described secular Title I service insofar as that prohibition rests on religious location, presumed inculcation from location or ordinary supervision of public personnel; if no independently supported legal basis sustains the onsite prohibition, it must be dissolved. Any remaining obligation must have an identified lawful basis, and any further proceedings must concern a specified material issue under the surviving law rather than require proof that future misconduct is impossible.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part IV of the Court's opinion; the same five Justices directly join this tailored relief and its public-control limits.

**Controlling explanation:** Rufo connects modification to the proven change while preserving the lawful purposes of continuing relief. The change today eliminates the categorical site-and-monitoring ground. The district court therefore cannot simply reinstate that same ground, presume that public service and religious instruction are inseparable, or treat ordinary secular supervision as itself forbidden. It must remove the prohibition to that extent. Supported questions about actual religious instruction, diversion, religious control of public employees or another independent legal prohibition remain subject to ordinary procedure and the governing law; hypothetical misconduct does not justify preserving Aguilar's rejected inference.

No additional negotiated obligation is presumed. If an independent statutory, contractual or decree obligation is established, constitutional permission does not by itself extinguish it. Existing lawful services may continue while the mandate is implemented promptly. Children currently receiving those services should continue to receive them during implementation, with existing statutory duties preserved and no new service entitlement created. Any implementation period must address identified tasks and circumstances; the Court sets no number of days, and administrative delay cannot indefinitely preserve the rejected categorical prohibition. The district court modifies the decree under the mandate. Until it acts, the City must obey the operative injunction.

The holding permits the described program; it does not command onsite delivery, create an unconditional funding entitlement, approve a replacement program, award damages or repayment, cancel contracts, or excuse past disobedience. It makes no finding that every present implementation satisfies the required secular controls.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Rufo's suitable-tailoring requirement controls implementation and preserves any independently lawful obligation. Its consent-decree rule is not converted into a presumption that this adjudicated injunction contains a voluntary stricter undertaking.

For the Lemon test and the onsite secular-service rule in Holdings I–II, see First Amendment, “Public instruction on religious-school premises,” Agostini, June 23, 1997, Nos. 96-552 and 96-553.

### Prospective officer suits and disputed tribal property rights

#### Idaho v. Coeur d’Alene Tribe of Idaho

**Docket or dockets:** No. 94-1474.
**Decided:** June 23, 1997.

##### A disputed federal tribal title question does not create a sovereign-lands exception to prospective officer relief

**Holding:** The Eleventh Amendment does not bar a claim for prospective declaratory or injunctive relief against officials actually connected to an alleged ongoing violation of federal tribal property rights merely because adjudicating that violation requires resolving a disputed federal title question or affects important sovereign interests. The decree must address proved unlawful future official conduct and may not bind the immune State's title, award damages or retrospective compensation, execute against state property or require the State itself to convey title.

**Proposition-level authority:** Stone-Zsela's Opinion of the Court, joined by Stevens, Souter, Ginsburg and Breyer: five Justices.

**Material application:** The pleaded land-regulating officials have a concrete connection to the alleged interference; a general law-obedience duty would not suffice. The actual request for exclusive use and exclusion of regulation is acknowledged, but the Ninth Circuit withheld a decree binding Idaho’s title and the case remains at the pleading stage.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Neither generalized state-interest balancing nor a special sovereign-lands exception bars the route. The Tribe must establish the federal right, ongoing violation and lawful remedy. No ownership, navigability, prestatehood disposition or separate United States action is adjudicated; no general Article I abrogation rule is announced.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Affirm permission for the prospective-officer claims, 5–4; leave the State, agency and direct quiet-title or retrospective-relief dismissals undisturbed, 9–0. Further merits and tailored-remedy proceedings are permitted, without automatic injunction or property transfer.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Young supplies the connected-officer route; Tindal and Treasure Salvors distinguish officer conduct relief from sovereign title. Oneida supplies federal-question character. Edelman, Green and Pennhurst retain retrospective and state-law limits. Seminole’s actual IGRA officer holding is consistent but supplies neither a universal statutory route nor an abrogation rule, and no IGRA sequence is imported.

### Review and authority of consent decrees

#### Lawyer v. Department of Justice

**Docket or dockets:** No. 95-2024.

**Decided:** 1997-06-25.

For the governing-law error and confined remand of the districting decree, see Lawyer v. Department of Justice, No. 95-2024, decided 1997-06-25, Election Law — Districting consent decrees and preserved claims.

##### I. The personal challenge to this decree remains reviewable

**Holding:** A voter with an established personal relationship to the challenged electoral assignment and decree may obtain review of the decree and the disposition of his own claims even when his design-only constitutional theory fails. This three-judge court's grant of districting injunctive relief is directly reviewable under 28 U.S.C. §§2284 and 1253; neither receipt of part of the requested practical relief nor failure on that substantive theory eliminates the controversy.

**Proposition-level authority:** Breyer's signed Opinion of the Court, Parts I–IV, is joined in full by Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. Eight Justices directly support every rule and qualification below. The opinion applies the existing Shaw, Miller and Vera rule as governing law; it does not attribute renewed substantive endorsement of that rule to O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy or Thomas. No combination of separate writings or narrowest-ground inference supplies the Court's authority.

**Limits and questions not reached:** This is no general resident-standing rule. The Court orders no Article III, mootness or insubstantiality dismissal.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Miller and Vera separate personal electoral assignment from substantive success and support direct review. Lujan retains personal injury, causation and redressability; Goosby's narrow insubstantiality exception does not convert this merits failure into jurisdictional dismissal.

##### II. General consent authority does not settle every districting-authority objection

**Holding:** A lawful consent decree may impose obligations broader than the relief obtainable after a trial without invariably requiring a prior adjudication of liability, but it must resolve a dispute within subject-matter jurisdiction, remain within the pleadings' general scope, advance the underlying law's objectives and comply with law. One litigant cannot veto the other parties' lawful settlement merely by withholding consent; their agreement nevertheless cannot extinguish his properly raised valid claim or impose duties on him without consent.

**Proposition-level authority:** Breyer's signed Opinion of the Court, Parts I–IV, is joined in full by Stevens, O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas and Ginsburg. Eight Justices directly support every rule and qualification below. The opinion applies the existing Shaw, Miller and Vera rule as governing law; it does not attribute renewed substantive endorsement of that rule to O'Connor, Scalia, Kennedy or Thomas. No combination of separate writings or narrowest-ground inference supplies the Court's authority.

**Material application:** Authorized litigation representatives made a genuine state settlement choice, but the March 19, 1996 judicial decree gave Plan 386 compulsory force. No legislative joint resolution or superseding state judgment adopting it is established.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The Court does not decide whether every federal districting decree needs an established violation or whether authorized litigation settlement always satisfies the State's redistricting opportunity. Neither four-Justice concurrence's additional position controls, and Stone's dissent supplies no fifth judgment-supporting vote for either.

**Treatment of earlier authority:** Firefighters applies with its jurisdiction, pleading-scope, underlying-law, legality and nonconsenter conditions; its general settlement latitude does not decide all reapportionment objections. Growe requires respect for actual timely state action, without converting a proposal into enactment. Richards and Matsushita distinguish opportunity to object from lawful authority over retained rights.

### Qualified-immunity preservation under Rule 50

#### McCardle v. Haddad

**Docket or dockets:** Second Circuit Nos. 96-9133(L) and 96-9503; no separate Supreme Court docket stated.

**Decided:** 1998-01-29.

##### The trial motion did not preserve search immunity

**Holding:** Rule 50 requires the trial motion to identify the judgment sought and its supporting legal and factual grounds; postverdict renewal does not ordinarily permit a new ground omitted from that motion. Functional presentation, including adequate explicit incorporation, can suffice without a particular label, but pleading qualified immunity and invoking it for the stop did not preserve the distinct search-immunity ground here; no demonstrated exceptional injustice requires reaching that ground.

**Proposition-level authority:** Opinion of the Court (Ginsburg), unanimous.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Search legality and immunity for a reasonable legal mistake are distinct. The motion expressly invoked stop immunity but identified no search-immunity ground or sufficient incorporation. The plaintiff-favorable search record supplies no compelled judgment establishing exceptional injustice; no new search-merits or ultimate immunity holding follows. The outer limits of exceptional review remain open.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Affirmed insofar as reviewed, leaving the unlawful-search verdict and $1 nominal judgment undisturbed. No immunity remand or new trial issues. The unappealed stop verdict and separate punitive and fee rulings are outside review; their reasoning is not adopted.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Gomez is applied to distinguish an affirmative immunity defense from an obligation courts must supply without its proper presentation.
- Elder is applied to preserve review of all relevant law on a preserved immunity issue; it does not revive an omitted ground or admit a new factual record.
- Harlow and Anderson retain objective, particularized conduct-date immunity. The Court neither grants nor denies that defense on its merits.
- Long informs the rejection of claimed exceptional injustice at its actual protective-search scope; neither a valid stop nor the officer's subjective account alone establishes a lawful search.
- Crawford-El and Fletcher retain ordinary procedure and the distinction between constitutional liability and personal defenses. Their holdings create no immunity-specific Rule 50 exception or motive element for this search claim.
- Johnson, Behrens, Clanton and Smith retain their legal/factual and claim-specific limits. Their interlocutory settings do not restrict this final-judgment channel or enlarge the trial motion. Lanier and later immunity decisions supply no notice retrospectively for the March 1992 conduct.
- Allentown's distinction between a fairly included argument and an independent unpreserved objection remains confined to its actual question and governing statute; Rule 50 controls here.
- Farrar and Hensley remain unchanged. Their explained assessment of actual success permits zero without mechanical proportionality or a categorical nominal-relief presumption. The Court does not approve the lower fee rationale or decide a fee amount.

### State-tax postpayment remedies

#### Newsweek, Inc. v. Florida Department of Revenue

**Docket or dockets:** No. 97-663.

**Decided:** 1998-02-23.

##### A clearly offered postpayment remedy cannot be withdrawn after payment

**Holding:** When a State clearly holds out a postpayment procedure for recovering unconstitutionally collected taxes, due process forbids denying that opportunity after payment solely because the taxpayer could have pursued an adequate prepayment challenge. The State must afford the promised clear and certain postpayment opportunity, subject to lawful reasonable conditions announced in advance and ordinary proof of payment and amount; neither automatic repayment of every dollar nor a particular equivalent correction is prescribed.

**Proposition-level authority:** Part I of the per curiam opinion, joined by all nine Justices. Part II's vacatur and remand is supported by the eight Associates; Stone-Zsela would reverse and remand under the same rule.

**Material application:** Florida’s established §215.26 constitutional refund route covered the tax payments at issue. Even assuming meaningful prepayment review under §72.011, the unused option does not answer the independent prohibition on withdrawing the offered postpayment route.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The existing state invalidity judgment supplies the tax premise. No new First Amendment tax holding, automatic refund, interest award, independent procedural default, general estoppel rule or reopening of a final judgment is decided.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Vacatur and remand require the constitutionally adequate postpayment opportunity under lawful reasonable preannounced conditions. The Court leaves payment proof, amount and lawful equivalent correction for adjudication.

**Material judgment:** All nine join the governing rule. The eight Associates vacate and remand; Stone-Zsela would reverse and remand under the same rule.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- Reich directly governs the withdrawal of an offered postpayment route and remains distinct from the adequacy of unused prepayment review.
- McKesson's compelled-payment rule is distinguished, not enlarged into automatic refund entitlement; Reich independently suffices even assuming meaningful prepayment review.
- Fulton preserves constitutionally adequate lawful remedial choices, subject to the separate McKesson and Reich predicates.
- Camps Newfound preserves open remedial choices without authorizing denial of all adequate correction.
- Lunding applies the same prepayment/postpayment distinction and ordinary conditions; its separate substantive tax holding is not extended here.

### Sovereign claims to an unpossessed maritime res

#### California v. Deep Sea Research, Inc.

**Docket or dockets:** No. 96-1400.

**Decided:** 1998-04-22.

##### A bare title assertion does not bar this in-rem admiralty action

**Holding:** The Eleventh Amendment does not bar federal in-rem admiralty jurisdiction over a wreck outside a State's possession merely because the State asserts title. The court may adjudicate the competing interests under governing law, and its lawful in-rem determination may bind the State's claimed interest; jurisdiction itself establishes no claimant's ownership.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's Parts I–II, joined by all eight other Justices, provide the unanimous controlling rule.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Claimed title and statutory constructive possession do not establish actual custody. No waiver through limited appearance, congressional abrogation, personal damages, coercive officer relief, seizure of State-held property or general in-rem exception is found.

**Operative remedy or transition:** In-rem jurisdiction is affirmed unanimously. See this case under Maritime Law for the independent abandonment/coverage inquiry and vacatur of premature title-related conclusions.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- The Davis supplies the actual-possession distinction in the treatment of a sovereign's claimed maritime property interest.
- Ex parte New York (No. 2) is distinguished because the State possessed and used the vessel in governmental service; its State-custody question is not decided here.
- Treasure Salvors is read within its State-held-artifact and arrest-warrant setting, not as a universal bar to a binding determination of an unpossessed res.
- Coeur d'Alene retains its officer-action and remedy limits; the distinct maritime route permits the binding in-rem adjudication recognized here.
- Seminole's actual officer holding and unresolved direct-State rationale are not enlarged; this decision adopts no general Article I abrogation rule and does not disturb Union Gas.

### Prison consent-decree termination and current proof

#### Benjamin v. Jacobson

**Docket or dockets:** Second Circuit No. 96-7957; no separate Supreme Court docket supplied.
**Decided:** 1998-05-07.

##### 1. The enacted conditions determine which prospective duties continue

**Holding:** Section 3626(b)(2) permits termination of covered prospective prison relief granted without substantive findings of necessity, narrowness and least intrusiveness; the movant must establish that predicate, and actual sufficient findings need no ritual statutory wording. Under amended §3626(b)(3), relief remains only on written, record-based findings that it is necessary to correct a current **and** ongoing federal-right violation, extends no further than necessary, is narrowly drawn, and is the least intrusive means necessary to correct the violation.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's Parts I–III and V have all nine Justices; that common coalition governs Holdings 1–3 and 5. Holding 4 identifies the distinct eight-Justice ground.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Consent decrees are covered. Relief beyond the minimum substantive conduct rule may remain when every statutory finding supports it; neither consent nor missing prescribed words decides termination. The amended conjunction applies to this pending case, and the unsupported remainder must end. Rufo is a separate route, not an added statutory prerequisite.

##### 2. Termination reaches future judicial obligations without erasing completed effects

**Holding:** Termination ends the decree's unsupported prospective judicial duties themselves, not merely one forum's power to enforce identical continuing duties. It neither retrospectively declares the original decree erroneous nor erases completed determinations, payments or other lawful historical effects; a truly independent state-law agreement or accrued claim requires its own legal basis.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Section 3626(d) preserves state-law relief without creating an independent agreement or claim. No contract, breach, accrued liability or proper enforcement forum is decided.

##### 3. The presented constitutional objections do not invalidate this framework

**Holding:** Congress may alter the conditions for prospective prison relief while courts independently determine violations, factual eligibility and remedial scope; this framework does not adjudicate the original dispute under unchanged law or cancel accrued damages and completed effects. Beneficiaries have no perpetual right to unchanged future enforcement, and the presented remedial classification satisfies ordinary rational review within the protections preserved below.

**Limits and questions not reached:** A supported claim excluded solely by the amended conjunction, an accrued independent claim, and necessary protection against a supported threatened injury remain unresolved. The framework preserves independent adjudication, meaningful remedies and pretrial detainees’ protection against punishment.

##### 4. The assumed independent contract-impairment objection supplies no additional bar

**Holding:** Assuming for this objection alone an independent contract and substantial impairment, a rational federal restriction on continuing supervision is permissible where the United States is not a contracting party. Federal judicial approval and legislation benefiting States do not themselves make the United States a party to the bargain.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's Part IV is joined by Stevens, O'Connor, Kennedy, Souter, Thomas, Ginsburg and Breyer, supplying eight votes for the rationale. Stone-Zsela agrees only in rejecting the objection.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The contract and impairment are assumed solely for this objection; neither an actual agreement nor cancellation of accrued liability is established.

##### 5. This record requires a genuine opportunity to present current proof

**Holding:** Where a court treats the existing record's silence as dispositive while legally refusing relevant current evidence necessary to the §3626(b)(3) inquiry, it must permit a meaningful evidentiary presentation and decide the statutory conditions on that record. This case-specific correction neither establishes a violation nor requires a new hearing in every termination proceeding.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The actual refusal of necessary current proof distinguishes this case from a speculative demand for further inquiry. No present violation, universal hearing duty or automatic continuation follows.

**Operative remedy or transition:** Affirmed in part, vacated in part, and remanded unanimously. The automatic survival of identical decree duties for state enforcement is vacated. The lower courts must allow necessary current proof, determine the amended preservation findings, and terminate unsupported prospective duties. Completed lawful effects remain; automatic stay and prisoner-release issues are not adjudicated.

**Treatment of earlier authority:**

- **Rouse:** Applied to the amended termination framework, prospective duties, completed effects and constitutional objections; distinguished on the documented refusal of current evidence.
- **Plyler and Lewis v. Casey:** Preserve the independent statutory route, remedial safeguards, statutory scope and meaningful access under the text each construed; their original conjunction is not rewritten.
- **Rufo:** Retains significant-change and tailored-modification requirements for its distinct equitable route, without becoming an additional statutory prerequisite.
- **Kokkonen:** Preserves the distinction between judicial commands and separate agreements and the need for independent enforcement jurisdiction.
- **Morgan Stanley and Plaut:** Their defined securities-dismissal reinstatement holdings supply neither absolute immunity for future supervision nor a general congressional reopening power.
- **Robertson; Wheeling Bridge; Klein:** Preserve the distinction between changed governing law and legislative adjudication under unchanged law.
- **Beach; Heller; Romer:** Supply rational review with an actual relationship between classification and legitimate ends.
- **Bell v. Wolfish:** Preserves the independent substantive protection of pretrial detainees against punishment.
- **National Railroad Passenger Corp. v. Atchison:** Supplies the federal-nonparty contract framework applied only on the assumed-contract premise.

### Prison consent-decree termination after constitutional reversal

#### Gavin v. Branstad

**Docket or dockets:** Eighth Circuit Nos. 96-3746 and 96-3748; no separate Supreme Court docket supplied.
**Decided:** 1998-05-21.

##### 1. The amended statutory conditions govern prospective relief

**Holding:** Section 3626(b)(2) permits termination of covered prospective prison relief granted without findings that the relief was narrowly drawn, went no further than correction of the federal-right violation required, and used the least intrusive means necessary for that correction; the movant must establish this original-findings predicate, and sufficient substantive findings need no ritual wording. Amended §3626(b)(3) preserves relief only on written, record-based findings that it is necessary to correct a current **and** ongoing federal-right violation, extends no further than necessary, is narrowly drawn, and is the least intrusive means necessary to correct the violation.

**Proposition-level authority:** Scalia's Parts I–III and V are joined by all nine participating Justices and control Holdings 1–3 and 5; Part IV's distinct coalition is stated in Holding 4.

**Limits and questions not reached:** The November 26, 1997 amendment governs this pending case. The statutory route is independent of Rufo; consent, relief beyond the minimum conduct rule and absent original findings do not establish present compliance or continuation. Each retained obligation needs the preservation findings.

##### 2. Prospective termination does not erase completed effects

**Holding:** Lawful termination ends unsupported future judicial obligations themselves, not merely federal enforcement while identical decree duties automatically survive as state contracts. It does not invalidate completed determinations, payments, or other lawful historical effects; independent agreements and accrued claims require their own legal basis.

**Limits and questions not reached:** Section 3626(d) creates no independent state contract or claim; existence, accrued liability, breach and enforcement forum remain unadjudicated.

##### 3. The presented constitutional objections fail

**Holding:** Congress may alter prospective remedial conditions while courts independently decide eligibility, violations, and relief; beneficiaries possess no permanent entitlement to unchanged supervision. The presented classification rationally relates the actual remedial restriction to legitimate institutional and resource interests while preserving meaningful remedies for federal violations.
